# Inferring Braid Requirements

## Workstream Metadata

- Kind: focused priority packet
- Status: active pre-derivation requirements program
- Created: 2026-08-22
- Claim level: methodological inference with explicitly graded requirements and speculative realizations
- Integration owner: [Mapping Electromagnetism](priorities.md)
- Exact equation and score owner: [Equation Mapping](../mapping-equations/priorities.md)
- Independent benchmark owner: [Mapping Benchmarks](../mapping-benchmarks/priorities.md)
- Retained-geometry and dynamics owners: Braid Program, Master Equation Closure, Photon Closure, Standard Model Closure, and the EOM solver
- Candidate adjudication owner: [Braid Candidate Requirement Adjudication](braid-candidate-requirement-adjudication.md)

## Purpose

This packet is the focused reverse-engineering home for a central $\mathbb{A}\mathbb{A}\mathbb{A}$ question:

> Given a tested observer-level behavior, equation, or benchmark, what must be true of the source assemblies, matter braids, Noether sea braids, causal-wake history, propagating carriers, and receiver assemblies for that behavior to emerge?

The immediate application is to effective electric and magnetic fields. The goal is not to assign $\mathbf E$ or $\mathbf B$ to an architrino or to rename an unknown sea configuration as a field. The goal is to infer the smallest native requirement bundle whose observer projection has the properties called electric and magnetic behavior.

The same method also supplies cross-sector clues for Standard Model assembly geometry. Frequencies, mode counts, masses, transformation laws, form factors, moments, polarization ranks, lifetimes, branching fractions, null channels, and controlled differences between related particles are treated as inverse constraints on recurrence, frame, exposure, topology, association, basin, and reaction-corridor records. This cross-sector use remains priority-only and does not transfer equation-score or retained-geometry ownership into this packet.

This is broader than a Noether sea-only study. A complete explanation may require geometry in several roles:

1. the emitting or maintaining source assembly;
2. the matter assembly whose state is being studied;
3. nearby, environmental, and extended Noether sea populations;
4. a propagating assembly, photon-channel packet, or changing causal-history record when the situation is dynamic;
5. the receiving or measuring assembly; and
6. the causal boundary history entering the declared region.

Plainly: start with what the effective behavior requires, then determine which braids must possess which geometry. Do not begin by choosing a favorite braid and asking how many observations can be described with it.

## Program Rationale

Tested effective behavior can constrain native geometry before a microscopic braid architecture is selected. Every measured symmetry, reversal, scaling law, null, range dependence, and conservation account removes candidate matter-and-sea geometries that cannot produce it. The resulting admissible-set intersection provides a disciplined complement to direct geometry construction.

The required external constraint surfaces are owned by:

- [Equation Mapping](../mapping-equations/priorities.md) owns equations such as `EQ-13` Maxwell and wave recovery, `EQ-26A` fine-structure coupling and running, and `EQ-27` magnetic moment and precession.
- [Mapping Benchmarks](../mapping-benchmarks/priorities.md) owns independent comparison situations such as the [Zeeman effect](../mapping-benchmarks/zeeman-effect.md), [Aharonov--Bohm effect](../mapping-benchmarks/aharonov-bohm-effect.md), [quantum Hall effect](../mapping-benchmarks/quantum-hall-effect.md), and magnetic-response and polarization tests.
- [Fine-Structure Coupling Map](fine-structure.md) develops one detailed same-record coupling application involving matter geometry, near-to-far sea state, action-period, photon-channel, exposure, and effective-field rows.
- [Fine-Grained Braid Maxwell Exploration](fine-grained-braid-maxwell-exploration-prompt.md) defines a finite-braid, equation-first residual protocol.
- [Mapping Electromagnetism Brainstorming](brainstorming.md) supplies provisional field-ontology, response-state, and behavior-to-geometry hypotheses for testing here.

This packet does not duplicate those inventories. It consumes their strongest constraints and records what they imply for admissible braid geometry.

Plainly: equations and benchmarks supply the constraints. This packet supplies the disciplined bridge from those effective facts to native braid requirements.

## Foundational Requirements

A physical situation requires a complete matter, sea, and boundary record rather than a probe label or an effective field name. The sea remains explicit until its contribution is derived or bounded as negligible. A conventional scale such as $\mu$ is an observer-level comparison coordinate, not a substrate cause, although the physical event realizing that scale may retune matter and the sea. A changed field-sensitive observable does not by itself establish a changed dimensionless coupling.

The Noether sea record requires near, environmental, and far partitions. In a trapped-electron determination, for example, the complete candidate record includes the electron braid, trap-source assemblies, sea near the electron, sea throughout the trap environment, the extended ambient sea, and incoming causal boundary history. A sea domain may be removed from the active model only after a declared null establishes that it is negligible in the tested regime.

Electric and magnetic fields are effective bulk summaries, not additional substances in the Euclidean void. Their native cause must descend from architrino paths, potential-bearing causal wakes, retained assemblies, and any induced Noether sea response. The still-unresolved direct-wake/sea allocation is stated precisely under [Theory-Layer Boundary](#theory-layer-boundary).

Capacitors, solenoids, toroids, switching events, and other controlled situations are discriminators when competing native interpretations require separation. They do not organize the inference program. The primary task is to define what effective $\mathbf E$ and $\mathbf B$ summarize in the native record; existing equation and benchmark packets constrain that definition.

Plainly: state what every successful geometry must produce before proposing what that geometry looks like.

## Theory-Layer Boundary

At the substrate layer, an architrino follows a path in Euclidean void and receives acceleration contributions from potential-bearing causal-wake intersections according to the Master Equation. It does not evaluate a Lorentz-force expression, carry a primitive electric or magnetic field, or interact with an autonomous field substance.

At the assembly and observer layers, stable regularities may be compressed into effective charge, current, electric and magnetic fields, potentials, constitutive coefficients, radiation fields, and coupling parameters. These are recovery targets and comparison variables. They may enter this packet only as behavioral requirements or projected outputs, never as microscopic premises.

The working ontology is therefore:

$$
\text{architrino paths and causal wakes}
\longrightarrow
\text{retained assembly, boundary, and declared sea record}
\longrightarrow
\text{effective observer record}.
$$

This ordering does not establish that every electromagnetic effect is mediated entirely by the Noether sea. Source assemblies emit causal-wake history directly, while the sea may reorient, deform, retune, screen, store, or propagate part of the resulting response. The relative shares must be derived.

Plainly: all effective fields must ultimately arise from native objects. No accepted derivation yet identifies the sea as the carrier, a constitutive participant, optional dressing, or a regime-dependent combination of these.

## Absolute Native Situation Record

For behavior family $k$ in a declared region $\Omega$, use the candidate absolute record

$$
\mathcal G_k(T;\Omega)
=
\left(
\Theta_{\mathrm{src},k},
\Theta_{\mathrm{matter},k},
\Theta_{\mathrm{sea},k}^{\mathrm{near}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{sea},k}^{\mathrm{env}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{sea},k}^{\mathrm{far}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{carrier},k},
\Theta_{\mathrm{recv},k},
\mathcal H_{\partial\Omega,k}
\right).
$$

The entries have role meanings rather than different ontologies:

- $\Theta_{\mathrm{src},k}$ records the assembly that emits or maintains the relevant causal history.
- $\Theta_{\mathrm{matter},k}$ records the matter assembly whose retained state or transition is under study.
- The three sea entries distinguish locally coupled response, response across the effective-field environment, and extended ambient or boundary response.
- $\Theta_{\mathrm{carrier},k}$ records a propagating packet or resolved disturbance when one exists; it may be empty in a stationary description.
- $\Theta_{\mathrm{recv},k}$ records the assembly that converts the environment into a measurable response.
- $\mathcal H_{\partial\Omega,k}$ records causal history entering the selected region from outside it.

One physical assembly may occupy more than one role. The role labels organize the event ledger; they do not create new particle species or authorize a field input.

Plainly: describe what the source, studied matter, surrounding sea, possible carrier, receiver, and boundary are actually doing before compressing them into an effective field description.

## The Inference Direction

For each observer behavior $k$, define a requirement bundle

$$
\mathcal Q_k
=
\left(
\mathcal O_k,
\mathcal T_k,
\mathcal D_k,
\mathcal L_k
\right),
$$

where:

- $\mathcal O_k$ is the measured observable or effective equation term with its uncertainty and covariance;
- $\mathcal T_k$ is its behavior under rotation, reflection, polarity conjugation, source reversal, receiver reversal, and history reversal where meaningful;
- $\mathcal D_k$ is its dependence on distance, time, frequency, geometry, preparation, boundaries, and operating regime; and
- $\mathcal L_k$ is its identity, action, energy, momentum, angular-momentum, wake, and Noether sea ledger.

A candidate geometry is tested only in the forward direction:

$$
\widehat{\mathcal Q}_k
=
\Pi_k[\mathcal G_k],
$$

with residual

$$
\mathcal R_k
=
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{obs},k}
+
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{transform},k}
+
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{ledger},k}
+
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{retune},k}.
$$

The admissible set for that behavior is

$$
\mathfrak A_k(\epsilon_k)
=
\left\{
\mathcal G_k:
\mathcal R_k\leq\epsilon_k
\right\}.
$$

The objective is not a geometry that explains one row. It is one shared record family in the intersection

$$
\mathfrak A_{\mathrm{shared}}
=
\bigcap_k
\mathfrak A_k(\epsilon_k),
$$

with no case-private retuning of its primitive or constitutive rules.

Plainly: each equation or experiment removes incompatible geometries. The surviving candidate must reproduce magnitudes, reversals, nulls, scaling, and balanced accounts across several independent constraints.

## Observable-To-Native-Motion Inference

### Geometry-Clue Taxonomy

An effective equation or observation can constrain braid geometry through several logically different channels. The inverse-mapping program should tag each row before proposing a realization:

| Clue class | Examples | Weakest geometry implication | Common overreach |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Inventory and counting | charge units, particle/conjugate pairs, three color choices, two photon polarizations, three generation classes | discrete identities, protected counts, response rank, or a finite family of branch classes | treating an effective count as a literal number of internal orbits or constituents |
| Recurrence and phase | clock rate, spectral line, oscillation, interference, sideband | coherent return, propagating phase, phase difference, transition cadence, or event statistics | calling every inverse-time readout an orbit |
| Orientation and transformation | parity, conjugation, helicity, chirality, spinor return, exchange sign | ordered frame, mirror class, polarity map, history lift, or exchange path | encoding several different transformations with one circulation sign |
| Spatial exposure | form factors, radii, multipoles, scattering angles, polarization transfer | channel-dependent moments, anisotropy, exposed scale, or resolved substructure | treating one projected density as a photograph of the full braid |
| Response rank and mode content | scalar/vector/tensor behavior, transverse/longitudinal modes, birefringence | number and transformation type of independent response coordinates | identifying an effective representation with one unique substrate shape |
| Reaction and basin topology | thresholds, branching, widths, confinement, hadronization, pair production | admissible graph surgery, basin boundaries, product corridors, and conserved identity routes | reading an arrow between particle labels as a microscopic mechanism |
| Environmental and amplitude response | Stark/Zeeman shifts, polarizability, nonlinear vacuum response, matter effects, dense packing | deformation coordinates, exposure retuning, coupled sea response, and first branch boundary | assigning the response entirely to matter or entirely to the sea without controls |

These clue classes must intersect. A candidate selected only by a count, one frequency, one symmetry label, or one form factor remains highly nonunique. The best candidates are those for which the same $\mathcal M_A$ record supplies several classes without changing its law, inventory, quotient, or exposure definition.

Plainly: frequency is one route to geometry, not the route. Counts tell us what must be distinguished; symmetries tell us how it transforms; scattering tells us what is spatially exposed; reactions tell us how the structure can change.

### Recurrence Depth

An observer-level frequency does not uniquely imply an architrino orbit. Let $\Theta_T$ denote the complete retained native history ending at absolute time $T$, let $q$ quotient only declared rigid translations, rigid rotations, label permutations, and other physical equivalences, and let $\Pi_k$ be the fixed observer projection for behavior $k$. For candidate period $\tau=2\pi/\omega$, define three recurrence residuals:

$$
R_{\mathrm{obs},k}(\tau)
=
d_k\!\left(
\Pi_k[\Theta_{T+\tau}],
\Pi_k[\Theta_T]
\right),
$$

$$
R_{\mathrm{shape}}(\tau)
=
d_q\!\left(
q(\Theta_{T+\tau}),
q(\Theta_T)
\right),
$$

$$
R_{\mathrm{hist}}(\tau)
=
d_H\!\left(
\Theta_{[T+\tau-\Delta,T+\tau]},
\mathsf S_\tau\Theta_{[T-\Delta,T]}
\right),
$$

where $\mathsf S_\tau$ shifts the comparison history by one candidate period and $\Delta$ covers the declared causal-memory horizon. Small $R_{\mathrm{obs},k}$ establishes only recurrence of the measured output. Small $R_{\mathrm{shape}}$ adds recurrence of the assembly geometry after the declared quotient. Small $R_{\mathrm{hist}}$ is the strongest row: the delayed history itself repeats and can support a periodic retained branch after the ordinary retention gates also pass.

Plainly: a spectral line can repeat even when the entire assembly does not. Before calling the motion an orbit, check whether only the detector signal, the assembly shape, or the full delayed history actually returns.

### Candidate Motion Coordinates

A geometry search should expose at least these independent coordinates rather than encode every periodic observation as a layer frequency:

| Candidate native motion | Minimal coordinate | Characteristic clue | Decisive discriminator |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Internal circulation or orbit | phase angle $\theta_a(T)$ with nonzero winding | recurrence with oriented angular-momentum and circulation reversal | fixed radii, phase advance around a closed path, and sign reversal under circulation reversal |
| Breathing or radial pulsation | scale coordinate $r_a(T)$ | scalar size or exposure modulation with no required net winding | correlated radial extrema across sites, reflection-even response, and near-zero circulation row |
| Rigid-frame precession | ordered frame $F_A(T)\in SO(3)$ | rotating axis while internal distances remain approximately fixed | orientation sidebands with stable body-frame shape and a separately closed angular-momentum ledger |
| Nutation or rocking | polar frame angle $\beta_A(T)$ | oscillating tilt superposed on circulation or precession | periodic polar-angle change and paired sidebands rather than uniform azimuthal advance |
| Twist or torsional mode | signed twist density $\vartheta(s,T)$ | handed phase gradient, chirality, or CP-sensitive response | mirror reversal changes the signed row while scalar cadence and total inventory stay fixed |
| Traveling internal mode | phase field $\phi(s,T)$ | spatially ordered phase lag and propagation | phase delay scales with separation along the assembly rather than all sites moving in phase |
| Exchange or permutation cycle | path in labeled configuration space | observable returns before every constituent label returns | nontrivial permutation after one output period and full identity return only after multiple periods |
| Beat or relative-clock mode | two or more phases $\theta_a-\theta_b$ | envelope modulation or oscillation length | independently measurable carrier rates whose difference predicts the slow recurrence |
| Intermittent basin return | return-section coordinate and dwell time | broad or state-dependent recurrence, switching, or metastability | hysteresis, dwell-time distribution, or first-exit corridor rather than a stable phase circle |
| Source or ensemble cadence | event times $\{T_n\}$ | repeated emission, collision, or population exchange | no single receiver history has shape recurrence although the event ensemble has a stable rate |

These coordinates may coexist. For example, a circulating assembly can precess, breathe, and carry a traveling phase wave. The inverse task is to use symmetry, harmonics, spatial phase, angular momentum, linewidth, and reaction provenance to determine which coordinates are actually required.

Plainly: orbit, breathing, twisting, rocking, and repeated events can share a frequency. Their spatial motion, reversal parity, sidebands, and identity return are different, so those extra observations are the useful geometry clues.

### Spectral And Temporal Clue Grammar

| Effective clue | Weakest native implication | Nonunique candidate realizations | Additional row needed to discriminate geometry |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sharp persistent line | coherent phase advance or recurrent source/readout over the coherence window | periodic orbit, normal mode, repeated transition, synchronized event train | recurrence depth, spatial phase, line polarization, and source depletion |
| Harmonic series | nonlinear periodic projection or nonsinusoidal recurrent motion | eccentric circulation, anharmonic breathing, clipped exposure, repeated impulses | harmonic parity and phase under reflection, circulation reversal, and amplitude change |
| Sidebands or multiplet | at least two coupled rates or one rate modulated by another coordinate | orbit plus precession, carrier plus breathing, split modes, boundary modulation | sideband spacing, polarization, orientation, and whether internal distances change |
| Resonant amplitude and phase lag | a coupled native or collective mode with finite storage and return | matter mode, sea mode, boundary mode, or hybrid | direct/sea knockout, spatial mode shape, energy-transfer ledger, and fixed-law amplitude sweep |
| Oscillation between readout classes | at least two coherent phase contributions or repeated basin exchange | relative-phase beat, tunneling-like first-exit cycle, alternating exposure projection | path-length scaling, preparation phase, coherence loss, and constituent-identity history |
| Finite linewidth | limited coherence, branch lifetime, environmental variation, or detector convolution | phase diffusion, metastable escape, collision broadening, unresolved mode family | independent source/detector resolution plus native survival and first-exit records |
| Lifetime or decay rate | finite-window survival measure and an admissible outgoing corridor | unstable manifold, collision channel, fold transition, environment-assisted escape | product identity, branching fractions, preparation dependence, and full event ledger |
| Frequency shift under environment | the environment changes phase advance, recurrence, or the observer projection | geometry deformation, sea dressing, boundary response, clock/readout change | same-history internal coordinates and observer projection before and after the environment change |
| Scalar response peak | a rotation-invariant changing coordinate or isotropically averaged mode | breathing, density/cadence modulation, orientation ensemble, composite threshold | orientation dependence, angular-momentum row, and resolved radial or tensor deformation |

No row may infer an internal speed from frequency alone. A speed inference requires a same-record path length and proof that the measured period belongs to traversal of that path rather than to precession, beating, projection, or an event cadence.

Plainly: spectra tell us that something is coherent, recurrent, coupled, or short-lived. Geometry appears only after the same record says what moved, over what path, with what phase relation, and for how long.

### Five Native Inverse-Time Classes

The inverse problem must classify an observed inverse-time quantity before it assigns a motion to it. The current equation, benchmark, braid, reaction, and Standard Model owners separate into four non-equivalent classes:

| Class | Native burden | Typical effective readout | Geometry that is *not* implied |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Formation completion | a prepared reaction history first enters a declared identity basin and satisfies its hold/ledger predicate | assembly formation time, hadronization ordering, settling or capture time | the formed object's internal period or later lifetime |
| Persistent relative recurrence | one retained history returns to the same reduced state, possibly modulo an independently justified rigid motion, phase convention, or label permutation | material clock, orientation cycle, protected action cycle | a literal circular orbit |
| Propagating phase cadence | a carrier advances a coherent phase through source, path, and receiver records | photon frequency, interference phase, packet helicity | a rest-frame clock or retained matter-braid orbit |
| Transition or gap readout | reproducible pre-event and post-event basins connect through one provenance-complete event | atomic line, absorption frequency, threshold | either basin circulating at the emitted frequency |
| Escape or event cadence | a metastable parent basin has a survival distribution and classified exits | lifetime, linewidth, branching fraction, repeated source cadence | a periodic internal motion with period equal to the lifetime |

For a native observable $O_\alpha(T)$, a useful diagnostic decomposition is

$$
O_\alpha(T)
=
\overline O_\alpha
+
\sum_{\mathbf m\in\mathbb Z^r}
A_{\alpha\mathbf m}
e^{i(\mathbf m\cdot\boldsymbol\Omega T+\phi_{\alpha\mathbf m})}
+
\sum_k a_{\alpha k}\psi_\alpha(T-T_k)
+
\eta_\alpha(T).
$$

The first sum is coherent recurrent or quasiperiodic content, the second is a sequence of transitions or exits, and $\eta_\alpha$ is unresolved or noncoherent variation. This is an analysis grammar, not a constitutive law. It must not be used to manufacture a frequency absent from the native record.

Plainly: a formation time, spectral line, photon frequency, precession frequency, and particle lifetime all carry time information, but they arise from different kinds of history. Classify the history before drawing the braid.

### Count And Response-Rank Clues

Observed state counts constrain the dimension and symmetry of an effective response space more directly than they constrain constituent inventory:

| Effective count | Geometry burden | What the count does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| two free photon polarizations | a rank-two transverse response space with no accepted free longitudinal channel | two architrinos, two loops, or the proposed planar pair |
| two Stern--Gerlach outcomes for a spin-$\tfrac12$ preparation | an apparatus-coupled orientation record with two stable outcome basins and the correct preparation-angle measure | two internal sites or a binary mechanical switch |
| visible $2\pi$ return and $4\pi$ spinor restoration | a nontrivial two-sheet history lift on a retained non-gauge row | an ordinary rotating loop or a visual belt-trick analogy |
| three color labels | a three-choice exceptional-axis or equivalent internal basis whose singlet composites close | three spatial colors, three clocks, or three free quark branches |
| eight gluon comparison modes | the traceless operator space on a three-state color basis, $3^2-1=8$ | eight standalone substrate particles or eight oscillation frequencies |
| three neutrino propagation modes | a three-dimensional coherent comparison space with two independent phase-rate gaps | three literal constituent binaries; that remains a candidate realization |
| three charged-fermion generations | three reproducible branch classes with common charge rules and distinct mass/exposure/lifetime records | the current three-, two-, and one-support shielding picture unless dynamics selects it |
| scalar, massless-vector, and massive-vector response classes | respectively a rotation-invariant channel, a rank-two transverse channel, and an effective vector channel that includes a longitudinal comparison mode | a unique radial braid, planar photon, or rigid three-axis weak-boson assembly |

Count recovery should therefore proceed in two steps: derive the native state or response space, then derive why the observer quotient sees exactly the stated number of distinguishable modes. Constituent counts enter only when identity and reaction ledgers independently require them.

Plainly: “two modes” and “two pieces” are different statements. Mode counts tell us how many independent ways the assembly can respond; inventory ledgers tell us how many architrino identities are present.

### Mass, Action, And Scale Clues

Mass and action supply important constraints, but they must not be collapsed into an assumed internal clock. The [Particle Masses](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/particle-masses.md) owner supplies the mass-map thesis. The following is a diagnostic restatement for this inverse-geometry packet, not a newly derived or independently accepted mass equation:

$$
m_{\mathrm{tr}}(A)
\sim
\alpha_{\mathrm m}
E_{\mathrm{internal}}(A)
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\mathcal Z_A\mathcal M_{\mathrm{sea},+}
\right],
$$

where $\mathcal Z_A$ is the branch-derived exposure/shielding tensor and $\mathcal M_{\mathrm{sea},+}$ is the reversible symmetric sea-response row. This requires a closed internal history and a reproducible response, but the scalar mass value alone does not reveal whether the internal motion is an orbit, breathing mode, quasiperiodic network, or statistically stationary retained state.

| Effective clue | Weakest geometry implication | What remains nonunique | Decisive additional row |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| nonzero stable rest mass | retained internal action/energy ledger with nonzero scalar exposure through the local sea response | motion type, path length, and total hidden internal energy | branch retention, exposure extraction, response tensor, and same-record acceleration response |
| particle/antiparticle equal mass | mass-facing geometry depends on complete $C$-even branch data while electric exposure conjugates | detailed site placement and motion order | exact whole-history $C$ sibling plus scalar mass and charge-response rows |
| large generation mass hierarchy at equal charge | branch classes differ strongly in shielding, internal ledger, sector energy, or sea response while keeping one charge rule | depleted supports, different topology, different cadence, or different exposure can all contribute | one shared mass map across lepton, up-type, and down-type families with no per-family retuning |
| $E=h\nu$ for a photon packet | packet energy and propagating phase cadence share one protected action scale | no photon rest clock and no necessary matter-braid orbit at $\nu$ | source/path/receiver packet ledger and an independently derived action unit |
| protected action quantum | a closed action-bearing history or event cycle has a geometry-derived invariant such as $h_\vartheta=\oint\vartheta_{\mathrm{PC}}$ | period alone does not fix action; equal-frequency families can carry different action | retained orbit/event, phase-space or Poincare-Cartan row, perturbation stability, and cross-family universality |
| Compton or inverse-mass comparison length | observer mass and phase localization define an inverse scale | not necessarily braid radius, orbit circumference, site separation, or exclusion envelope | branch-derived size, form factor, localization, and action rows on one assembly |
| de Broglie wavelength or momentum phase | translating assembly or packet carries coherent spatial phase relative to group momentum | not an internal constituent orbit and not necessarily the rest-state cadence | same-record group motion, phase gradient, clock/ruler extraction, and recoil |
| threshold energy | an admissible basin boundary, corridor opening, relock, breakup, or product-formation cost exists | barrier height does not identify the coordinate crossing it | pre/post basins, first crossing coordinate, products, recoil, and complete ledger |
| scale-dependent effective coupling | exposure, wake/sea dressing, accessible channels, or quotient changes with probe scale | need not mean the retained core changes shape with every scale | one scale-labelled carrier with threshold inventory and fixed scheme/projection |
| Koide-type charged-lepton relation | after an independent mass map exists, the three branch outputs may occupy a constrained mass-root surface | no direct constituent count, angle, knot, or generation mechanism follows from the relation | data-blind branch-derived masses tested afterward; failure must not be repaired by tuning the geometry to the relation |

The strongest action warning is exact: frequency by itself cannot derive $h$. A family may keep one cadence while its path amplitude or response normalization changes the action continuously. Universality therefore requires a protected action invariant or transition unit on several independently derived branches, not one fitted product $E/\nu$.

Plainly: mass tells us how strongly a retained internal ledger is exposed, and action tells us what is protected per cycle or transaction. Neither number by itself tells us the architrinos' path.

### Scalar Mass Response With An Axial Moment Constrains Motion

A fermion-like assembly may expose an ordered spin/magnetic axis while its leading rest-state mass-facing and electric monopole responses remain approximately rotation invariant. This combination is more informative than either row alone. It requires the same geometry to support an axial projection without leaking unacceptable orientation dependence into every scalar-facing projection.

For one accepted branch and fixed probe window $W$, let $M_{ij}^{(A)}(W)$ be the observer-level linear group-response tensor extracted from a complete source--assembly--sea history in the declared weak regime. It is a recovered assembly response, not an architrino mass tensor. Define

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{iso}}^{(A)}(W)
=
\frac{
\left\|
M^{(A)}(W)
-
\frac{\operatorname{tr}M^{(A)}(W)}{3}I
\right\|_F
}{
\left\|M^{(A)}(W)\right\|_F
}.
$$

The scalar-facing target requires $\Delta_{\mathrm{iso}}^{(A)}(W)\le\epsilon_{\mathrm{iso}}$ on the declared rest/weak-response domain, while an independent axial moment or ordered-frame readout remains nonzero. The zero-denominator case abstains rather than passing.

Plainly: the particle can have a direction that a magnetic or spin-sensitive experiment sees, but ordinary scalar response must not reveal a large easy-versus-hard acceleration axis. The geometry needs a reason those two measurements see different parts of the same history.

Four native mechanisms remain distinguishable:

| Isotropy mechanism | Required geometry | Distinctive prediction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| static high symmetry | a volumetric arrangement whose scalar response tensor is isotropic while a separate framed/history row carries the axial moment | isotropy persists down to windows shorter than any internal orientation cycle, subject to source-resolution limits |
| dynamic orientation averaging | an anisotropic instantaneous geometry that regularly precesses, nutates, permutes, or otherwise samples orientations | anisotropy decreases with averaging window and leaves native sidebands or phase structure near the orientation cadence |
| sector-projection cancellation | several anisotropic constituents or layers cancel in the scalar projection but add in the axial/current projection | scalar and magnetic tomography have different spatial/mode weights on the same fixed history |
| coupled sea compensation | matter anisotropy is offset by a derived anisotropic near-sea response in the scalar channel | frozen-sea and responsive-sea controls separate the cancellation and require a closed matter--sea ledger |

An unprepared ensemble of randomly oriented particles is not automatically a single-assembly explanation. If one prepared particle retains an ordered moment over the measurement window, ensemble averaging cannot by itself prove that its scalar response is isotropic. The same prepared history must be tested under rotated source and apparatus records.

Dynamic averaging gives a direct frequency clue. Let $\omega_{\mathrm{ori}}$ denote the resolved internal orientation cadence and $\tau_{\mathrm{probe}}$ the source/receiver response window. The heuristic separation

$$
\omega_{\mathrm{ori}}\tau_{\mathrm{probe}}\gg1
$$

can suppress the window-averaged trace-free response, but it is not itself a proof. The executable test is the measured dependence

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{iso}}^{(A)}
=
\Delta_{\mathrm{iso}}^{(A)}
\left(
\omega_{\mathrm{drv}},
W,
\hat{\mathbf n}_{\mathrm{src}},
\hat{\mathbf n}_{\mathrm{frame}}
\right)
$$

under a fixed-law drive-frequency and orientation scan. A true dynamic-average mechanism must expose the predicted crossover or spectral content without retuning the branch or response projection.

This does not yet imply high architrino speed. A cadence constrains a ratio of path-history change to absolute time. Converting it to a constituent speed requires the actual path length or spatial excursion on the same retained record. Conversely, if a finite path scale is independently bounded below and the averaging cadence is bounded below, then their product supplies a native speed bound.

The scalar/axial intersection also constrains electric deformation. A weak electric-like source may produce small internal stretching while leaving the charge index and leading scalar response unchanged. The first measurable effect could be a phase shift, trace-free deformation, or changed exposure rather than a new charge or large group-level anisotropy. Stronger drive may then reveal mode coupling or a branch boundary in the same tomography record.

Claim grade: the coexistence of scalar-facing isotropy and a nonzero axial response is a deduced recovery constraint. Static symmetry, dynamic averaging, projection cancellation, and sea compensation are inferred alternatives. Falsifier: one accepted prepared branch exhibits scalar anisotropy above the frozen bound, or a proposed dynamic-average mechanism has no window/frequency dependence and no resolved internal orientation history.

### Magnetic Moment Must Co-Locate Charge Current And Angular History

The EQ-15/EQ-27 and [Zeeman](../mapping-benchmarks/zeeman-effect.md) owners require the effective magnetic moment, spinor return, precession, spectral response, leading charged-lepton ratio, and anomalous residual to arise on one retained history. The geometry must therefore relate a charge-facing current moment to an independently closed angular-momentum record.

For a prepared charged branch $A$, define the effective moment $\boldsymbol\mu_A[W]$ from its derived exposed current and let $\mathbf J_A[W]$ be the observer angular record recovered from matter, relative motion, corridor, wake, and sea rows. On the nonzero spin subspace, a comparison map has the form

$$
\boldsymbol\mu_A
=
\frac{q_A^{\mathrm{label}}}{2m_A^{\mathrm{obs}}}
\mathsf G_A
\mathbf J_A,
$$

where $q_A^{\mathrm{label}}$ and $m_A^{\mathrm{obs}}$ are already recovered observer quantities and $\mathsf G_A$ is a dimensionless same-record moment map. This equation is an effective normalization target, not an architrino-level mass or force law. A candidate leading charged-lepton branch must recover the declared near-$2I$ action of $\mathsf G_A$ on its spin subspace, with

$$
\mathsf G_A
=
2I+\delta\mathsf G_A,
$$

and derive the anomalous residual $\delta\mathsf G_A$ from the same fixed history, exposure, wake, sea, and projection law.

Plainly: the geometry must explain why the part seen as electric current and the part seen as spin have a precise leading relationship. Naming one loop “charge” and another “spin” without deriving their ratio misses the clue.

The cross-particle controls are:

| Comparison | Required common row | Required difference or transformation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| electron versus positron | scalar mass response, leading $\mathsf G$, spinor class, and conjugate basin structure | whole-history conjugation reverses charge-facing current and moment consistently while preserving the normalized leading ratio |
| electron versus muon/tau | common charge quantum, spinor class, and leading moment grammar | mass/shielding, lifetime, spatial exposure, and anomalous residual may differ without a private leading law |
| proton versus neutron | confined spin-$\tfrac12$ angular grammar and color closure | neutron net charge vanishes while internal signed current and magnetic moment remain nonzero |
| charged lepton versus scalar channel | nonzero charge may exist in either comparison role | a rotation-scalar spin-$0$ response has no unprepared permanent axial moment, while the fermion retains one |
| static moment versus driven/radiative response | same exposed-current and angular history | radiation, loss, or absorption may add a symmetric/dissipative channel but cannot redefine the static moment map |

Plainly: conjugates test sign, lepton generations test the common leading rule, the neutron tests internal counterflow without net charge, and a scalar channel tests whether the axial moment really belongs to the fermion history.

The neutron row is an exact anti-aliasing clue. Net charge is the spatial integral of signed exposure; magnetic moment is a first moment of signed current. Cancellation of the first does not cancel the second. Any candidate that computes moment solely as net charge times one global rotation rate therefore fails neutral-composite response.

Four native moment carriers remain live:

1. exposed constituent circulation on a retained path;
2. distributed counterflow whose net charge cancels partly or wholly while its current moment remains;
3. ordered-frame or corridor holonomy whose evolved charge-facing response projects as a moment; and
4. a matter current dressed by a derived wake/sea response that supplies the anomalous residual without changing the charge index.

The mechanisms can coexist, but each contribution must be separable by constituent-current, circulation-reversal, frame-reversal, direct/sea, and window/frequency controls. A fit coefficient called an anomalous moment is not a geometry.

The most useful executable residual is a non-aggregated vector rather than a sequential or weighted score:

$$
\boldsymbol{\mathcal R}_{\mu J}^{(A)}
=
\left(
\Delta_{\mathrm{align}},
\Delta_{\mathsf G\to2I},
\Delta_{\delta\mathsf G},
\Delta_C,
\Delta_{\mathrm{ledger}}
\right),
$$

with every component reported separately and no scalar pass/fail cancellation. The owner freezes normalizations and tolerances before comparing lepton generations or conjugates.

Claim grade: co-location of moment, angular history, conjugation, leading ratio, and anomaly is a deduced recovery requirement. The four carriers are inferred alternatives. Falsifier: separate fitted charge-current and spin geometries reproduce the joint comparisons without a common identity, orientation, or ledger map, or a neutral composite cannot retain a nonzero current moment after its charge integral cancels.

### Couplings Constrain Sector Projections

A dimensionless coupling is most useful as a constraint on how one native branch is exposed to one effective sector, not as a direct measure of the branch's full size or shape. A candidate inverse-map grammar is

$$
\Pi_S:\mathcal H_{\mathcal B}\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{d_S},
$$

$$
\mathfrak c_S(\mu\mid\Theta_\star)
=
\mathcal N_S(h_\vartheta,c_\gamma)
\mathcal Q_S\!\left[\Pi_S(\mathcal B)\right]
\mathcal K_S
\left(
\mu;\rho_{\mathrm{NS}},\chi_{\mathrm{sea}},I_\mu
\right).
$$

$\Pi_S$ is a sector-facing projection, $d_S$ is the effective operator-space dimension, $\mathcal Q_S$ is an exposed-inventory or exposure quotient, $\mathcal K_S$ is a scale- and environment-dependent response, $I_\mu$ is the declared resolved-threshold inventory, and $\mathcal N_S$ carries shared normalizations such as the action-period and photon-speed rows when applicable. This factorization is a diagnostic proposal; it does not assert that physical couplings literally factor this way.

For a fixed-background running comparison, the componentwise no-retuning condition is

$$
\partial_{\ln\mu}\mathcal B
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}\Pi_S
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}h_\vartheta
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}q_{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}c_\gamma
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}\rho_{\mathrm{NS}}
=
\partial_{\ln\mu}\chi_{\mathrm{sea}}
=0.
$$

The comparison scheme label is held fixed rather than differentiated. Only the declared response and threshold inventory may vary. The conventional scale $\mu$ is not automatically a braid radius, internal cadence, or physical shrinkage of the electron.

Plainly: keep the particle, projection rule, normalization, and background fixed while changing what the probe resolves. Running should come from the declared response and accessible channels, not from quietly rebuilding the particle at each scale.

The resulting universality target is conditional but sharp. If a family $\mathcal F$ shares one sector-facing motif, then on the comparison domain

$$
\Pi_S(\mathcal B_f)
\cong
\Pi_S(\mathcal B_{f'}),
\qquad
\mathcal N_S^{(f)}
=
\mathcal N_S^{(f')}
\qquad
\text{for all }f,f'\in\mathcal F,
$$

while orthogonal shielding, internal-history, mass-facing, and exit-corridor coordinates may differ. A family-indexed effective charge or privately changed projection rejects the shared motif.

Several cross-coupling comparisons are especially selective:

1. **Electric universality versus mass, lifetime, and scalar hierarchy.** The charge-facing row remains common across $e,\mu,\tau$ while shielding, mass, lifetime, and the scalar derivative vary. One scalar exposure number cannot control all four.
2. **Fine structure versus running versus magnetic response.** Exposed charge and action period anchor the reference coupling; running belongs to response and threshold rows; leading $g$ belongs to ordered-frame topology; and the anomaly belongs to residual dressing. Changing the action unit or charge inventory to fit the latter rows fails.
3. **Weak universality versus flavor overlap and anomaly bookkeeping.** One weak-exposure domain must serve charged-current handedness, $W/Z$ response, CKM/PMNS overlap, and the inherited quark/lepton multiplicity constraints. The observer matrices are not literal spatial rotations.
4. **Electromagnetic running versus strong running and confinement.** Opposite scale trends require distinct sector response kernels, while high-resolution strong response and long-distance open/closed color-corridor behavior must still descend from one strong-sector carrier. A universal scalar screening fraction is too weak.
5. **Scalar coupling versus $W/Z$ normalization and mass exposure.** The most economical scalar row is the derivative of the already fixed mass response on the same electroweak/sea record. Per-particle scalar charges or a separate scalar sea reject that factorization.

Charge quantization itself constrains a discrete exposed-inventory grammar and the dynamic exclusion of unused stable classes; it does not prove six exposed axial sites or their placement. Gauge-anomaly cancellation constrains the joint effective multiplicity and charge table, but inherited cancellation does not select a retained geometry. High-energy coupling unification remains an optional downstream comparison until the electromagnetic, weak, and strong carriers are separately recovered.

Plainly: coupling data tell us which parts of a reusable architecture must be shared and which projections must remain distinct. They do not draw the braid, but they reject any architecture that fits each interaction with a private exposure rule.

### Chirality Is Not Massive-Particle Helicity

The effective weak-handedness label and observer helicity coincide only in restricted high-speed limits; they are not the same geometry coordinate for a massive retained assembly. Helicity is an observer projection of angular history along group momentum. Chirality is the weak-representation/exposure row that determines which charged-current corridor is available. An observer change can reverse the momentum direction assigned to a massive particle without physically rebuilding its weak-facing branch.

For nonzero observer momentum, define the helicity comparison

$$
h_{\mathrm{obs}}
=
\operatorname{sgn}
\left(
\mathbf J_{\mathrm{obs}}\cdot\mathbf p_{\mathrm{obs}}
\right),
$$

while the weak-facing record is a physical event projection

$$
\mathcal E_W
=
\mathcal E_W
\left[
\Theta_{\mathrm{fermion}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{source/app}},
\mathcal N_{\mathrm{sea}},
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{wake}}
\right].
$$

At $\mathbf p_{\mathrm{obs}}=0$, helicity is undefined, but the retained branch and its possible weak docking geometry do not cease to exist. Under a pure change of observer reconstruction of one physical history, $h_{\mathrm{obs}}$ may reverse for a massive branch while the native event record and its covariantly transformed $\mathcal E_W$ remain the same physical record.

Plainly: an observer who describes the particle as moving the other way may reverse its helicity label. That observer change cannot expose or hide a different set of architrinos inside the particle.

This creates a direct audit of the current [weak-coupling-triad candidate](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/fermions/quantum-number-mapping.md#the-weak-coupling-triad-geometry), whose provisional selector uses a propagation direction $\hat{\mathbf p}$. That construction remains viable only if the direction enters as a physical source--assembly--apparatus relation on the complete event record, or as a covariant shorthand derived from it. If an arbitrary observer momentum reversal changes which triad is called exposed, the candidate has aliased chirality with helicity.

The minimum coordinate split is

$$
\Theta_{\mathrm{fermion}}
\supset
\left(
\mathcal F_{\mathrm{spinor}},
\mathcal E_W^{\mathrm{posture}},
\mathbf V_{\mathrm{grp}},
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{event}}
\right),
$$

where the spinor/frame row, weak-exposure posture, group motion, and physical event relation are separately inspectable. They may be dynamically coupled, but none is defined solely as the sign of another.

| Control | What is changed | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| observer re-projection | observer chart only; native source, branch, sea, wake, and apparatus history fixed | effective helicity may relabel for a massive branch, but native weak exposure and event probability are covariant descriptions of the same event |
| physical group-motion reversal | source or preparation creates a different branch history relative to the same apparatus | any weak-response change must follow from the newly evolved relative geometry, not from a sign substitution |
| low-speed/rest continuation | group momentum approaches or reaches zero on an accepted massive branch | helicity loses its label at rest while the ordered frame, weak posture, and possible docking response remain well-defined |
| spatial parity sibling | complete positions, velocities, frames, roots, wakes, sea, and apparatus are mirrored | polar, axial, and weak-handed response rows transform under the declared effective parity action without changing polarity by definition |
| whole-history conjugate | polarities and conjugate event provenance transform at fixed spatial order | particle/antiparticle weak corridors transform through the conjugate record, independently of the helicity sign |
| near-massless propagation limit | one branch family is continued toward a regime where opposite-helicity content is suppressed | any chirality--helicity locking must emerge as a limiting projection rather than a substrate identity imposed on every massive branch |

This is also a test of Lorentz recovery from absolute-time dynamics. The substrate history is not boosted into another spacetime ontology. Instead, one native record must support observer reconstructions whose helicity labels and weak observables transform consistently without changing the branch, roots, or sea state merely because the observer moved.

Claim grade: the independence of massive-particle helicity and weak chirality is a deduced recovery requirement. The weak-exposure posture and physical source/apparatus relation are inferred native carriers. Falsifier: a velocity-sign-only weak selector reproduces rest, observer-reprojection, parity, conjugation, and high-speed limiting rows on one fixed physical history without hidden internal orientation or event geometry.

### Flavor Mixing Requires Weighted Geometry And A Cycle

CKM, PMNS, strong flavor preservation, neutral-current suppression, and $CP$-sensitive transport should be read as different outputs of one typed branch-and-corridor grammar. Let $F\in\{\ell,u,d,\nu\}$ label a physical family and $g\in\{1,2,3\}$ a branch or propagation mode. A candidate record is

$$
\Theta_F
=
\left(
\{B_{F,g}\}_{g=1}^{3},
\mathcal E_W|_F,
H_F^{\mathrm{res}},
\Gamma_F^W,
\Gamma_F^N,
\Gamma_F^S,
\mathcal L_F^{\mathrm{src/path/det}},
\mathcal C
\right).
$$

$B_{F,g}$ is a whole-history branch rather than a particle label, $\mathcal E_W$ is one universal weak-exposure rule, $H_F^{\mathrm{res}}$ is a family-specific phase or retention operator, the $\Gamma$ rows are charged- weak, neutral, and strong admissible path sets, $\mathcal L$ binds source, path, environment, and detector, and $\mathcal C$ is whole-history polarity conjugation.

`Same weak domain` must be type-correct. A quark and a neutrino cannot share one physical branch identity. They can inhabit different fibers of one rule:

$$
\mathcal D_W
=
\bigsqcup_F\mathcal D_W^{(F)},
\qquad
\mathcal E_W^{(F)}
=
\mathcal E_W|_{\mathcal D_W^{(F)}}.
$$

Within one event, every row uses the same physical branch and event record. Across families, branch carriers differ but the operator, normalization, conventions, and no-retuning rule remain common.

Plainly: the weak interaction can use one rule on different kinds of particle. Requiring one rule is not the same as pretending the particles share one physical history.

#### Bare Shielding-Distance No-Go

The proposed charged-fermion support vectors are

$$
\mathbf s_1=(1,1,1),
\qquad
\mathbf s_2=(1,1,0),
\qquad
\mathbf s_3=(1,0,0).
$$

Their unweighted Hamming distances obey

$$
d_H(\mathbf s_1,\mathbf s_2)
=
d_H(\mathbf s_2,\mathbf s_3)
=1.
$$

Therefore every overlap rule of the form $O_{ij}=F(d_H(\mathbf s_i,\mathbf s_j))$ predicts $O_{12}=O_{23}$. It cannot reproduce the effective CKM hierarchy in which the first--second overlap is much larger than the second--third overlap. This is a conditional mathematical no-go: the three bit strings may still label generations, but a successful overlap needs index-sensitive actions, orientation, phase, exposure, or corridor history beyond their bare distance.

Plainly: `111`, `110`, and `100` count three levels, but their simplest geometry makes the two adjacent steps identical. The observed mixing says those steps must carry additional structure.

#### Cycle-Rank Requirement For A Physical Holonomy

For a connected generation-transport graph,

$$
\beta_1
=
|E|-|V|+1
$$

counts independent cycles. The chain $1\leftrightarrow2\leftrightarrow3$ has $\beta_1=0$. Every edge phase can then be removed by rephasing its nodes, so the graph carries no invariant loop holonomy. A real phase-bearing $1\leftrightarrow3$ route, or another closed history loop, raises $\beta_1$ and can support a non-removable phase.

For a proposed effective path grammar,

$$
K^W_{ba}[e]
=
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_e^W(a\to b)}
w_\gamma
e^{i(\phi_\gamma^++\phi_\gamma^-)},
$$

whole-history conjugation reverses the conjugation-odd phase while preserving the even phase. Two interfering histories can then give a comparison-level asymmetry of the schematic form

$$
\Delta P_{a\to b}
=
-4
\sum_{\gamma<\gamma'}
w_\gamma w_{\gamma'}
\sin\Delta\phi_{\gamma\gamma'}^+
\sin\Delta\phi_{\gamma\gamma'}^-.
$$

This is a recovery grammar, not a claim that complex probability amplitudes are substrate primitives. Its exact structural consequence is that a one-path channel, a real overlap chart, or a cycle-free transport graph cannot carry the proposed $CP$ holonomy.

Plainly: a meaningful loop phase needs an actual loop and at least two routes whose relative phase can matter. A fitted angle attached to a three-node line does not supply either one.

#### Neutral-Current Scalar Condition

Let $N_F$ be the leading neutral-current operator on a three-generation family. If

$$
N_F=n_F I_{\mathrm{gen}},
$$

then for every weak-to-branch basis rotation $U_F$,

$$
U_F^\dagger N_FU_F
=
n_FI_{\mathrm{gen}}.
$$

Tree-level neutral flavor change is therefore absent. Loop-level change may still arise from event histories with non-identical generation-dependent charged-path contributions. This is a sufficient condition, not a uniqueness theorem, but it gives a sharp falsifier: a leading neutral corridor that sees generation geometry strongly enough to produce off-diagonal terms after the basis change violates the required suppression.

Neutrino oscillation adds a separate rank constraint. The traceless residual operator must carry three modes and two independent phase-rate gaps; a common phase, static offsets, or one gap cannot recover the propagation pattern. Charged-fermion shielding tiers and neutrino propagation modes may solve the same three-state counting problem through different physical realizations.

Plainly: charged currents must distinguish generation enough to mix it, neutral currents must be blind enough not to change it directly, and neutrinos must retain two independent phase clocks after the common clock cancels. One static three-bit label cannot perform all three jobs.

#### Flavor And Dipole Nulls Require One Factorized Record

The strongest flavor information comes from pairing an allowed route with a nearby suppressed route on the same family record. The present corpus covers ordinary muon decay and quark charged-current mixing through the [weak-visible branch packet](../mapping-equations/eq-16-weak-visible-branch-ledger-source-field-map.md), tree-level neutral-current diagonality and a formal GIM-like cancellation through the [common architecture](../mapping-equations/equation-common-architecture.md), and rare-kaon event bookkeeping, weak $CP$, and a quantitative neutron electric-dipole scaffold through their existing equation and benchmark owners. Coverage is only partial for the electron dipole and neutrinoless double beta decay, and no owned quantitative packet presently covers $\mu\to e\gamma$, $\mu\to3e$, coherent $\mu\to e$ conversion, or representative tau charged-lepton flavor change.

This gap does not license adding those observer processes as substrate laws. It identifies a missing same-record test of whether one generation geometry can allow charged-current flavor routing while suppressing direct charged-lepton relocking.

Plainly: we know how the framework proposes to allow some flavor changes, but we have not yet tested the same geometry against the strongest cases where a closely related flavor change is not seen.

A minimum common factorization requires distinct projections of one retained family:

$$
\left(
\Pi_W,
\Pi_N,
U_F,
U_\gamma^{\mathrm{hist}},
\mathbf d_A,
\mathcal C_L
\right).
$$

$\Pi_W$ is charged-current exposure, $\Pi_N$ is the leading neutral-current projection, $U_F$ is the recovered family-overlap map, $U_\gamma^{\mathrm{hist}}$ is conjugation-sensitive transport history, $\mathbf d_A$ is a static spin-aligned electric first moment, and $\mathcal C_L$ is the conjugation/lepton-number branch map. They are readouts of one branch family, not independent adjustable couplings.

The formal charged-lepton null diagnostic has the comparison structure

$$
\mathcal A_{\mu e}^{X}
\propto
\sum_k
U_{\mu k}U_{e k}^{*}g_k^X,
\qquad
X\in\{\gamma,3e,\text{conversion}\},
$$

where $g_k^X$ is an observer-level channel response to be recovered from the complete intermediate-history and apparatus or nuclear record. If the $g_k^X$ are identical, overlap unitarity cancels the common part. This is a recovery-row grammar, not a primitive loop law. Its geometric content is that the family paths must close coherently enough to cancel their common response, while small branch-dependent differences may survive in declared rare channels.

Plainly: the cancellation target is not a new microscopic loop law. It asks whether one complete family geometry makes the shared contribution cancel while leaving only the small, history-dependent differences that a rare route can expose.

The adjacent comparisons are:

| Allowed or observed row | Suppressed or null row | Minimum native distinction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $\mu\to e\bar\nu_e\nu_\mu$ | $\mu\to e\gamma$ and $\mu\to3e$ | the charged-current two-neutral corridor may change exposed flavor, while direct charged-lepton generation relocking has vanishing or extremely small leading support |
| ordinary muon capture | coherent $\mu\to e$ conversion | nuclear weak docking may absorb the ordinary transaction, while a final electron without the ordinary neutral provenance is suppressed by the same lepton-flavor kernel |
| CKM charged-current change | tree-level photon/$Z$ flavor change | $\Pi_W$ is non-diagonal between the selected bases while $\Pi_N$ remains diagonal at leading order |
| rare loop-level neutral flavor change | tree-level neutral flavor zero | a complete intermediate-family sum cancels the common response, leaving only branch-dependent history differences |
| weak flavor $CP$ | electron and neutron electric-dipole nulls | path holonomy may remain conjugation-odd while static spin-aligned electric first moments cancel in their sector projections |
| neutrino oscillation | neutrinoless double-beta null | flavor-phase transport need not open a self-conjugate lepton-number-changing association corridor |

A single scalar weak exposure cannot supply this table. The candidate needs a charged-current projection, a leading neutral projection, coherent intermediate-family closure, a transport-phase row, separate static moment quotients, and an explicit conjugation/lepton-number corridor. The parts may share one underlying geometry, but their transformations and null mechanisms must remain separately measurable.

No new benchmark owner is created here. The largest future coverage need is a single charged-lepton flavor-null packet anchored by ordinary muon decay and ordinary muon capture, followed by an electron-dipole owner parallel to the neutron scaffold and a quantitative neutrinoless-double-beta event ledger. Those obligation artifacts should be created only when a retained carrier or an active owner can consume them.

Plainly: one architecture can still explain all these flavor and dipole facts, but only if it has several disciplined projections. A separate fitted mechanism for each rare or forbidden process would conceal whether the common geometry actually works.

#### Three Generations Require A Discrete Branch Exhaustion Result

The observer statement that charged leptons, neutrinos, up-type quarks, and down-type quarks each organize into three generation rows is not proved by writing three candidate diagrams. The [Quantum Number Mapping](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/fermions/quantum-number-mapping.md#three-generation-closure-benchmark) owner already requires common representation bookkeeping and suppression of non-baseline partners. The inverse-geometry consequence is an exact basin or mode-count burden.

For sector $F$ and fixed representation-facing labels, let $\mathcal R_F$ be the set of accepted retained, propagating, or otherwise role-admissible complete histories, and let $Q_F$ remove only declared redundancies. Define the robust generation classes as positive-width connected components or spectrally isolated admissible modes of

$$
\mathfrak G_F
=
\mathcal R_F/Q_F.
$$

The generation-count target is

$$
N_{\mathrm{gen}}^{(F)}
=
\#\pi_0^{\mathrm{robust}}(\mathfrak G_F)
=3,
$$

or an explicitly equivalent three-mode statement when generation is carried by isolated modes of one connected branch rather than disconnected components. Every counted class needs a formation/preparation route, positive basin or mode margin, common charge/color/weak representation rows, and a distinct mass/exposure/mixing fingerprint. Every additional class must be absent, transient, confined into an accepted composite role, or below a declared observer bound.

Plainly: three sample parameter settings are not three generations. The dynamics must produce exactly three durable choices, show how each can be formed and recognized, and explain what happens to all other possible choices.

Five native mechanisms remain live and have different signatures:

| Candidate generation carrier | Required native signature | Discriminator |
| --- | --- | --- |
| topological or history index | three admissible discrete index values protected while closure/framing/root validity floors remain open | a generation change requires a classified singular, root-chart, or topology-changing event rather than smooth shielding drift |
| three dynamical basins of one continuous coordinate | three positive-width attractors or metastable basins separated by measured boundaries | perturbations return within a class, while finite crossings show hysteresis, barrier, or first-exit structure |
| three isolated modes of one reusable core | three orthogonal or Gram-resolved branch modes with distinct mass/exposure fingerprints | nonlinear continuation preserves a common core and reveals mode coupling, daughter basins, or loss at finite amplitude |
| three phase-lock or permutation classes | three robust relative-phase, holonomy, or identity-return patterns | observer recurrence, identity recurrence, cross-phase, and mixing overlap distinguish the classes even when gross support is similar |
| three support-tier lock states | three dynamically selected coherent-support subsets with complete identity and wake provenance | tier removal or decoherence changes mass/lifetime while charge, color, weak representation, and the remaining identities stay ledger-complete |

These mechanisms are not interchangeable. A topological index predicts no smooth within-family conversion without a validity-floor event. A multistable coordinate predicts basin boundaries. A mode picture predicts a common linear or nonlinear response basis after retention. A phase-lock picture predicts coherent cross-phases and return permutations. A support-tier picture must explain the provenance and dynamics of every depleted support rather than replace it with a dash in a label.

Cross-sector agreement adds another constraint. Let $g\in\{1,2,3\}$ be the recovered generation index and $F\in\{\ell,\nu,u,d\}$. The candidate may use different sector morphologies, but one alignment relation must bind their index ordering:

$$
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{gen}}
:
(F,g)
\longmapsto
\Theta_{F,g},
$$

with representation invariance

$$
\Pi_{\mathrm{rep},F}(\Theta_{F,g})
\simeq
\Pi_{\mathrm{rep},F}(\Theta_{F,g'})
$$

for all $g,g'$ in a fixed sector, while mass-facing, lifetime, and overlap rows remain allowed to differ. The alignment relation must not be defined after sorting observed masses. It needs a native index, formation order, support relation, phase class, or other data-blind correspondence.

This creates a no-continuum test. If the proposed generation coordinate is a continuous shielding amplitude $s$, then ordinary evolution must show why only three intervals or points of $s$ support admissible classes. Otherwise the model predicts a continuum of same-charge family partners. Likewise, three support bits do not close the program unless the fixed law rejects the unused subsets and the weighted overlap map reproduces unequal mixing.

Claim grade: exactly-three robust observer classes and no extra accepted partners are deduced recovery requirements. The five carrier mechanisms are inferred alternatives. Falsifier: a continuous or combinatorial candidate with no derived selection boundary nonetheless produces only the three observer classes under an exhaustive admissible search, or an accepted fourth class survives with the same representation rows and no observer counterpart.

### Neutral-Coherent Fermion Search Blueprint

The neutrino row supplies a particularly sharp inverse-geometry problem because the source and detector identify flavor-facing weak corridors while the path preserves a neutral coherent carrier. The useful native object is therefore not three particles repeatedly replacing one another. It is one complete source--path--detector history with a common propagating support and at least three resolved internal eigenrecords whose two independent relative phases can change along the path.

A minimum record is

$$
\Theta_{\nu}
=
\left(
\mathcal B_{\mathrm{neu}},
\mathcal U_{\mathrm{src}},
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{path}},
\mathcal U_{\mathrm{det}},
\boldsymbol\varphi_{\mathrm{rel}},
\mathcal E_W^{\mathrm{posture}},
\mathcal M_{\mathrm{env}},
\mathcal C,
\Gamma_{\mathrm{det}}
\right).
$$

Here $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{neu}}$ is the neutral propagating branch or branch family; $\mathcal U_{\mathrm{src}}$ and $\mathcal U_{\mathrm{det}}$ are the weak source and detector projections; $\mathcal H_{\mathrm{path}}$ is the fixed-law path-history evolution; $\boldsymbol\varphi_{\mathrm{rel}}$ contains two independent relative phases after the common phase is quotiented out; $\mathcal E_W^{\mathrm{posture}}$ is weak exposure rather than observer helicity; $\mathcal M_{\mathrm{env}}$ records matter-assembly and Noether-sea response along the path; $\mathcal C$ is whole-history conjugation; and $\Gamma_{\mathrm{det}}$ is the rare localized detector reaction corridor.

Plainly: the source prepares one combination of three internal travel modes, the modes accumulate different phases without becoming three separately observed particles, and the detector reads a new combination. The detector reaction is a separate event from the coherent propagation.

This record implies six geometry requirements.

1. **Common carrier support.** The three phase-bearing rows must remain parts, modes, locks, or quotient classes of one coherent neutral history. Three unrelated retained assemblies with fitted switching probabilities do not explain interference.
2. **Rank three after constraints.** The resolved comparison space must have three admissible modes and two independent phase-rate gaps after the common clock, normalization, and gauge-like redundancies are removed. Three named labels alone do not establish this rank.
3. **Nearly common translation with non-common internal phase.** A single group path must coexist with small mode-dependent phase accumulation. The geometry therefore needs a coordinate that changes its action or mass-facing response more strongly than its charge, color, and leading translational morphology.
4. **Weak projection at the endpoints.** Flavor belongs to the source and detector corridor overlaps, not necessarily to a permanently exposed spatial feature during free propagation.
5. **Environmental phase response without identity loss.** Matter may change the effective mode operator through assembly and sea history, but the same neutral inventory, branch identity, and phase provenance must pass through the region. An inserted observer-level potential is a recovery target, not a substrate premise.
6. **Coherence and detection are independent margins.** Long phase coherence constrains path-history stability, whereas weak detectability constrains the small measure of $\Gamma_{\mathrm{det}}$. Neither is a particle lifetime.

These requirements favor a soft internal coordinate on a stiff translating carrier: the gross support and neutrality are preserved while a small relative lock, permutation, orientation, exposure, or sea-dressing coordinate carries the phase differences. They disfavor geometries in which changing mode requires breaking and rebuilding the entire assembly at each oscillation.

Plainly: the carrier must be stable enough to travel, but internally delicate enough for several coherent phases to separate. That is a strong clue toward a shared scaffold with a low-cost relative motion, not three wholly different braids taking turns.

Four native realizations remain distinguishable:

| Candidate realization | Native phase carrier | Same-record discriminator | Fast rejection |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| three isolated modes of one neutral core | three eigenmotions of a common retained or propagating support | one fixed core, three Gram-resolved modes, nonlinear continuation, and two phase gaps | the reference support is not admissible, or fewer/more than three isolated modes survive |
| three relative-lock classes | three phase-locked relations between polarity-conjugate subrecords | lock angle, return map, and identity-preserving transitions remain measurable | a continuum of equivalent locks survives or transitions require assembly destruction |
| three identity-permutation modes | distinct return characters of identities on equivalent ports | visible-shape return differs from identity/history return and produces unequal overlaps | permutations are pure relabelings after the declared quotient or supply equal phase gaps only |
| common core with mode-dependent sea dressing | one core history coupled to three derived environmental response eigenrecords | vacuum and material continuations use one law while changing relative phases predictably | the sea modes were authored to fit the target or propagation loses common inventory |

The current near-photon polarity-conjugate pair is only one candidate support for this blueprint. Because its proposed photon base lock has not been exhibited, no residual distance from that lock is presently a measured native coordinate. The inverse requirements above remain usable without assuming the pair, planarity, or photon adjacency: neutrality, coherent rank, two phase gaps, endpoint weak projections, and matter-path response are the actual constraints.

#### Source--Path--Detector Geometry Experiment

One fixed-law comparison can separate the realizations without importing the observer oscillation equation as a primitive:

1. form or identify one admissible neutral carrier family and record its full identity, wake, root, frame, and near-sea history;
2. derive its admissible perturbation or branch basis only after retention or propagating-role validity is established;
3. quotient the common phase and verify exactly two independent relative phase-rate gaps across three accepted modes;
4. construct at least two endpoint weak-corridor projections from the same exposure law, with no mode-specific retuning;
5. propagate the same prepared combination through vacuum-like and matter-filled assembly/sea histories, preserving the complete provenance;
6. test path composition: two consecutive segments must give the same final record as their ordered composed evolution, including any noncommuting environmental response;
7. apply whole-history conjugation and physical parity separately, then verify the declared transformations of phase, posture, source, and detector rows;
8. open the detector corridor only at the receiver and ledger every product, recoil, sea change, and surviving or consumed neutral identity.

The decisive null controls are a common-phase-only carrier, a two-mode carrier, an accepted fourth mode, incoherent stochastic flavor hopping, and a path whose matter response is an inserted scalar rather than an evolved assembly/sea record. Each can mimic part of the observer pattern while failing the geometry burden.

A positive geometry clue would be one neutral support with three admissible internal modes whose common component controls translation while a small trace-free residual controls phase and endpoint overlap:

$$
H_{\nu}^{\mathrm{native}}
=
h_0 I_3
+
H_{\nu}^{\mathrm{rel}},
\qquad
\operatorname{tr}H_{\nu}^{\mathrm{rel}}=0,
\qquad
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathrm{gap}}
H_{\nu}^{\mathrm{rel}}=2.
$$

This is a comparison-level decomposition of a derived native operator, not a primitive quantum Hamiltonian. It says that the large shared motion cancels from flavor comparison while two small differences remain observable.

Plainly: most of the three modes may be the same geometry. The experiment is sensitive to the two small ways in which their clocks differ. That makes a near-degenerate internal-motion search more appropriate than searching first for three visibly different neutral braids.

Claim grade: common coherent propagation, three resolved modes, two independent gaps, endpoint flavor projection, and path-dependent matter response are deduced recovery requirements. A soft relative coordinate on a stiff common carrier is an inference from their intersection. The four realizations are candidate search families. Falsifier: a provenance-complete fixed-law history recovers the full source--path--detector pattern through incoherent replacement, one phase gap, or three unrelated carrier geometries without any shared support or equivalent coherence carrier.

### Transition Frequency Is Not Automatically An Internal Frequency

Let retained basins $A$ and $B$ be connected by an event that emits a carrier. The effective energy--frequency relation does not by itself establish either $\omega_\gamma=\Omega_A$ or $\omega_\gamma=\Omega_B$. A native relation becomes supportable only when one fixed record derives a selection rule such as

$$
\omega_\gamma
=
\mathbf m\cdot\boldsymbol\Omega_A
-
\mathbf n\cdot\boldsymbol\Omega_B
+
\omega_{\mathrm{path/medium}},
$$

while source depletion, basin change, emitted carrier, recoil, path, medium, receiver, and boundary ledgers close. The coefficients $\mathbf m$ and $\mathbf n$ must emerge from the admissible transaction and return geometry; they are not fitted quantum-number labels.

Plainly: an assembly can possess regular internal motion while emitting a different frequency. The emitted cadence can be a difference, harmonic, beat, or event-generated packet phase.

### Atomic Selection Graph As Geometry Tomography

Atomic spectra become substantially more informative when treated as a graph of allowed, suppressed, and alternative transition corridors rather than as a list of line frequencies. For parent basin $A$, daughter basin $B$, effective photon-channel class $c$, and prepared finite measure $\mu_T$, define the pre-detector transition incidence

$$
\mathsf M_{AB}^{(c)}(T)
=
\mu_T
\left\{
z\in\mathcal B_A:
\Phi_T(z)\in\mathcal B_{B+\gamma_c}
\right\}.
$$

The useful comparison colors are effective E1-like polarity-dipole exposure, M1-like exposed-current and ordered-frame response, E2-like rank-two polarity exposure, paired two-photon output, and nonradiative/remnant/medium routing. These names label observer-level channel ranks. They do not assert literal substrate dipoles, loops, quadrupoles, or pre-existing photons.

Then

$$
A\xrightarrow[c]{\mathsf M_{AB}^{(c)}>0}B
$$

denotes an admitted channel, while $\mathsf M_{AB}^{(c)}\simeq0$ suppresses only that declared color. A leading one-photon null does not imply that a two-photon, current-like, quadrupole-like, medium, or collision-assisted route is absent.

Plainly: the important question is not only how far apart two atomic states are. It is which geometrical route can connect them and what kind of carrier, recoil, and apparatus record that route produces.

The smallest high-discrimination comparison basis is:

| Shard | Controlled recovery row | Native coordinate isolated |
| --- | --- | --- |
| gross-line control | one common hydrogen carrier serving H-$\alpha$ and H-$\beta$ without per-line retuning | envelope functional, clock conversion, photon speed, recoil convention, and common response |
| leading allowed channel | hydrogen $2p\to1s$ one-photon row | nonzero leading polarity-dipole-like basin measure, envelope change, and transverse carrier ledger |
| leading null with higher-order rescue | hydrogen $2s\to1s$ leading one-photon suppression with nonzero two-photon continuum | parity-like/angular history and carrier multiplicity beyond the energy gap |
| hyperfine current-like control | the 21 cm $F=1\to F=0+\gamma$ row | coupled electron--nuclear ordered frames with nearly unchanged gross $1s$ spatial envelope |
| rank-two control | one source-backed quadrupole-dominated atomic transition with cascades separated | a genuine rank-two exposure channel distinct from polarity dipole and current response |
| degeneracy-lifting triad | fine, Lamb, and hyperfine splittings on linked states | separate orbital/spin-frame, wake/dressing, and nuclear-frame dependencies |
| field transformations | one base line under zero, signed electric-like, and signed magnetic-like source--sea histories with analyzer views | Stark polar deformation, Zeeman axial response, reversal parity, and photon polarization selection |

Plainly: this small set separates energy gaps from corridor geometry. A single retained atomic architecture would have to explain why one route is strong, another is suppressed, and another needs two carriers or a different response rank.

The [atomic precision packet](../mapping-equations/eq-26-31-observation-first-precision-packet.md) and [Zeeman benchmark](../mapping-benchmarks/zeeman-effect.md) own the currently mapped gross hydrogen, transition-event, two-photon, fine/Lamb/hyperfine, Zeeman, and general basin-measure grammar. The corpus does not yet contain an explicit atomic E1/M1/E2 comparison matrix, a dedicated Stark benchmark, or a source-backed atomic quadrupole case. Those are coverage gaps, not permission to import an orbital picture. The quadrupole slot should remain unpopulated until a primary comparison source and a retained carrier consumer are selected.

This comparison supplies several compact contradiction cores:

1. **Gap-only routing fails:** neighboring states can have comparable energy scales but radically different one-photon transition measures.
