2. **Spatial-envelope-only routing fails:** the 21 cm row changes coupled frame structure while the gross spatial envelope remains $1s$.
3. **One universal dipole corridor fails:** the two-photon, current-like, and rank-two channels require distinct exposure rank or event topology.
4. **Frequency-only fitting fails:** it cannot predict strength, suppression, polarization, branching, recoil, or lifetime.
5. **One correction bucket fails:** fine, Lamb, hyperfine, Zeeman, and Stark rows isolate different dependencies and cannot be absorbed into one gross cadence adjustment.
6. **Emission without recoil fails:** every accepted carrier must close source, product, recoil, medium, remnant, wake, and sea ledgers.

A later same-record packet should bind the parent/daughter basin identities, electron and nuclear ordered frames, mirror/parity-like history, exposed rank-one and rank-two polarity moments, current moment, channel incidences, photon Gates A/B/C, complete event ledger, field histories, detector/analyzer, and one no-hidden-retune witness. It would infer geometry from which edges exist and which vanish, not from the line-frequency labels alone.

Plainly: atomic transitions are a controlled way to ask the same hidden electron, nucleus, photon, and sea geometry several different questions. The pattern of allowed and missing edges is more revealing than fitting another spectral number.

### Candidate Morphology Record

Before particle names are assigned, a candidate branch should expose a common geometry record

$$
\mathcal M_A(T)
=
\left(
\mathcal I_A,
G_A,
d_{\mathrm{supp},A},
\{R_\ell,\omega_\ell,\phi_\ell,\hat{\mathbf n}_\ell\},
F_A,
\mathcal T_A,
\boldsymbol\zeta_A,
\pi_A,
\mathcal B_A,
\mathcal C_A
\right)_T.
$$

Here $\mathcal I_A$ is the polarity and identity inventory, $G_A$ is the active-root and closure graph, $d_{\mathrm{supp},A}$ records whether the occupied support is line-like, planar, or volumetric, the layer rows record scale, cadence, phase, and oriented normals, $F_A$ is the ordered frame, $\mathcal T_A$ records twist, writhe, linking, and handedness without collapsing them into one number, $\boldsymbol\zeta_A$ records sector-dependent exposure and shielding, $\pi_A$ records any nontrivial return permutation, $\mathcal B_A$ records retention and basin data, and $\mathcal C_A$ records any open corridor or classified reaction boundary. The coordinates are a proposed search record; their existence or independence is not assumed.

This record prevents several common aliases: equal inventory does not mean equal connectivity; equal cadence does not mean equal phase; equal external charge does not mean equal exposure; and equal observer identity does not mean that every constituent label returned after one observed period.

Plainly: describe what is present, how it is connected, where it sits, how it moves, what is exposed, and whether it actually returns. A particle label should be the output of that record, not a substitute for it.

### Retained-Branch Mode Atlas

The same-inventory comparisons suggest a mode-first search after a genuine retained core is found. A candidate must first be an accepted equilibrium, relative equilibrium, or periodic retained branch under ordinary EOM evolution. No stability spectrum or particle interpretation may be computed about a prescribed path or a configuration that is not actually occupied.

For an accepted periodic branch $\Theta_\star(T)$ with complete-history period $T_\star$, let $\Phi_{T_\star}$ be the delayed-history return map on the declared admissible perturbation space. The linear return operator is

$$
\mathcal M_\star
=
D\Phi_{T_\star}[\Theta_\star].
$$

Its multipliers and mode histories are meaningful only after removing exactly the declared translation, time-origin, and other genuine redundancy rows. Neutral modes required by those symmetries must not be called instabilities or particle excitations. For a stationary retained branch, the corresponding linear response operator is used only after exact acceleration balance has been certified.

Plainly: first prove that the candidate really persists. Only then ask how it can wiggle. A vibration analysis of a shape that immediately flies apart has no physical subject.

For each non-gauge mode $v_k$, record a mode fingerprint

$$
\mathfrak v_k
=
\left(
\omega_k,
\lambda_k,
\delta R_k,
\delta Q_k,
\delta\mathbf C_k,
\delta F_k,
\delta\mathcal T_k,
\delta\boldsymbol\zeta_k,
\delta\Gamma_k,
\delta\Theta_{\mathrm{sea},k}
\right),
$$

where $\omega_k$ is an oscillatory phase rate when one exists, $\lambda_k$ is growth/decay or return information, and the remaining rows give the mode's scale, shape, circulation, frame, twist, exposure, corridor, and sea content. These are mass-free native diagnostics. An observer mass or energy gap may be compared only after the assembly mass/action bridge is derived.

The nonlinear continuation then perturbs both signs and several amplitudes of each mode under one fixed law and classifies the outcome:

$$
\Theta_{\star,k}(0;a)
=
\operatorname{Retr}_{\Theta_\star(0)}(a v_k),
\qquad
a\in\{-a_m,\ldots,-a_1,a_1,\ldots,a_m\}.
$$

Here $\operatorname{Retr}$ is a declared local chart or retraction onto the admissible history manifold. Every initial record must pass polarity, persistent-identity, speed, and prehistory checks before evolution. Each continuation is labeled retained return, nearby retained daughter, metastable resonance, mode coupling, topology or root-chart transition, classified product exit, or generic failure. The amplitude grid, prehistory, sea state, quotient, and tolerances are frozen before inspecting particle comparisons.

This atlas directly addresses several geometry clues:

| Mode-atlas pattern | Candidate inference | Required caution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| one mode changes ordered-frame/angular response while inventory and color closure stay fixed | possible $\pi/\rho$ or nucleon/$\Delta$-type excitation coordinate | total angular label does not identify whether the mode is circulation, precession, twist, or corridor motion |
| one isotropic mode changes scale/mass-facing response with little vector/tensor leakage | scalar or breathing-like response candidate | isotropic observer response does not prove literal radial constituent motion |
| a degenerate transverse pair carries relative phase and a propagation continuation | photon/vector-channel candidate | degeneracy alone does not prove a photon, helicity, retention status, or exchange class |
| a mode opens a finite first-exit corridor while the base remains retained | resonance/decay candidate | its oscillation rate and exit rate remain distinct |
| external effective-field drive overlaps selectively with one mode | Stark, Zeeman, polarization, or transition-selection clue | the overlap depends on the derived source--sea and exposure maps, not a primitive field coupling |
| two modes beat coherently while common phase cancels | flavor or neutrino-like phase candidate | beat frequency does not establish repeated particle conversion or a unique internal orbit |
| a finite-amplitude continuation changes root or topology class | candidate generation, reaction, or support transition | a chart change is not a new particle until the daughter basin and event ledger are certified |

Plainly: the perturbation must remain a lawful history, not merely a vector added to coordinates. Its nonlinear fate then says whether the mode returns, couples, crosses a genuine branch boundary, or simply destroys the candidate.

The strategic possibility is that some observer particle distinctions are different retained or metastable modes of reusable cores rather than unrelated constituent diagrams. The atlas tests that possibility without presuming it. Failure is equally useful: if no mode of a shared-inventory core can reproduce the paired angular, exposure, and exit differences, those particles require different retained morphology families.

Plainly: once a real braid is found, shake it in every independent lawful way and see which stable patterns, resonances, and exits it naturally supports. That is a disciplined route from one architecture to a particle family.

### Candidate Reusable-Motif Factorization

The most economical current hypothesis is not one unrelated shape for every observer particle. It is a factorization of each candidate morphology into a small number of reusable records:

$$
\mathfrak M_X
=
\left[
\mathcal C^{\,n_X}
\times
\mathcal D_X
\times
\mathcal F_X
\times
\mathcal G_X
\times
\mathcal U_X
\right]
/\mathcal Q_X.
$$

Here $n_X\in\mathbb N_0$ is the number of identity-bearing core records in the candidate, and each copy retains a distinct persistent-identity row inside the product. In addition:

- $\mathcal C$ is an identity-bearing core history record; it is not assumed to be one presently retained catalog braid or to contain the same number of coherent supports in every generation;
- $\mathcal D_X$ is an optional polarity-decoration and signed-inventory row;
- $\mathcal F_X$ is an indexed frame with sector-dependent exposure;
- $\mathcal G_X$ is the association, closure, or transaction-corridor graph;
- $\mathcal U_X$ records spatial support, response rank, propagation role, and retained, metastable, propagating, transient, or collective basin status; and
- $\mathcal Q_X$ quotients physically redundant axis names, frame relabelings, and pure label redundancies independently shown not to alter persistent identity, causal-root history, or reaction provenance.

This is an inferred factorization for search, not particle ontology and not a claim that the current candidate catalog is retained.

Plainly: reuse a few structural motifs, decorate and expose them differently, and connect one or more copies through different graphs. The particle families would then be recurring constructions rather than unrelated tiny machines.

The factorization organizes the present hypotheses as follows:

| Effective family | Candidate reuse | Coordinate that must vary | Principal falsifier |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| charged leptons and conjugates | one charged-fermion core family, six-unit decoration, whole-history conjugation | shielding coherence, weak exposure, and exit-basin access across generations | separate mass, lifetime, or conjugation laws are needed for each lepton |
| up- and down-type quarks | charged-fermion core plus one indexed exceptional-axis sector | polarity inventory, selected down-family branch, generation support | color rerouting changes flavor, charge, generation, or the selected down-family sector |
| mesons | matter/conjugate quark motifs plus a closed two-node color graph | relative phase, frame alignment, generation mismatch, and exit corridor | pion, kaon, and rho require new elementary motifs rather than modes and graph changes |
| baryons | three quark motifs plus a closed three-sector graph | flavor inventory, frame alignment, basin depth, and reaction exits | singlet bookkeeping does not eliminate free color or distinguish proton from unstable resonances |
| photon and neutral-lepton candidates | distinct locks of a polarity-conjugate pair motif | exact transverse propagation versus residual weak exposure and relative phase gaps | a retained photon, if found, has no compatible shared pair motif or the neutrino row erases required phase gaps |
| gluon comparison channel | color-exceptionality reconfiguration on an association graph | corridor twist, recoil, and sector routing | eight independent permanent substrate inventories are required rather than modes of a three-sector operator space |
| $W^\pm/Z$ comparison channels | localized weak transaction graph with temporary recruited participation | signed payload, longitudinal response, exposure, and exit window | permanent unledgered constituents are required or partial channels cannot reuse one corridor grammar |
| scalar/Higgs-like response | collective scalar response of the same mass-facing core/sea population | scalar medium coordinate and derivative of the mass-response map | family-specific scalar charges or uncontrolled vector/tensor leakage are required |

Several transformations must preserve the reusable records rather than replace them with particle-specific rules. Whole-history conjugation acts on polarity, paths, roots, wakes, action, and stability while leaving pro/anti orientation as an independent coordinate. Candidate generation surgery changes coherent support through

$$
111\leftrightarrow110\leftrightarrow100
$$

only if every added or removed identity is routed through reactants, products, or the Noether sea. Weak active-triad surgery carries the proposed signed six-unit transaction while preserving spectators. Strong-sector rerouting permutes exceptional-axis labels under one $S_3$ action while preserving total inventory, charge, flavor, generation, and the selected down-family sector.

Association then changes the graph rather than the constituent ontology:

$$
q\otimes\bar q
\longrightarrow
\mathcal G_{\mathrm{meson}},
\qquad
q_1\otimes q_2\otimes q_3
\longrightarrow
\mathcal G_{\mathrm{baryon}}.
$$

A scalar response is most economical when its effective coupling is a derivative of the same branch-derived mass response,

$$
g_{H,A}^{\mathrm{eff}}
=
\left.
\frac{\partial M_{\mathrm{sh}}(A;\theta,\varphi)}
{\partial\varphi}
\right|_{\varphi=0},
$$

rather than an independently fitted scalar charge for each family. Every one of these moves still requires ordinary Master-Equation evolution and complete reaction provenance.

Plainly: if the motifs are truly reusable, changing generation, color, weak routing, association, or scalar environment should modify only the declared coordinates. A hidden redesign of the entire particle for each case would falsify the factorization.

The largest danger is overgeneration. Six-site polarity combinatorics do not remove unused permutations, isolated fractional-charge branches, extra stable integer-charge assemblies, or the two candidate down-type dyad families. The three-state color algebra does not establish eight retained gluon objects. Temporary $W/Z$ transaction inventory is not permanent constituent inventory. The current photon-neutrino relation lacks a retained photon base point. The meson/baryon graph grammar must also admit and then classify higher multiquark singlets rather than inventing a new primitive for each observed exotic candidate.

The missing theorem is therefore a **selection result**: under one common law and sea regime, the observed combinations must possess retained, propagating, or transaction basins while the unobserved combinations are absent, transient, confined, or below their experimental bounds. The factorization fails if that selection requires private coefficients or exceptions for each species.

Plainly: a compact construction kit is useful only if dynamics explains why a small subset of its possible constructions exists. Otherwise it merely creates a larger unexplained particle zoo.

The current corpus supports the following role distinctions. Every candidate picture in this table remains subordinate to retained-branch and event-ledger proof.

### Controlled Particle Comparisons

The most useful inverse clues often come from two observer rows that share most labels but differ sharply in one response. Suppose candidate morphologies are factorized provisionally as

$$
\mathcal M_A
=
\mathcal M(\mathcal S,\mathbf u_A),
\qquad
\mathcal M_B
=
\mathcal M(\mathcal S,\mathbf u_B),
$$

where $\mathcal S$ is the declared shared subrecord and $\mathbf u$ contains the coordinates allowed to differ. A controlled comparison asks whether

$$
\Delta\mathcal O_{A/B}
=
\mathcal O[\mathcal M(\mathcal S,\mathbf u_A)]
-
\mathcal O[\mathcal M(\mathcal S,\mathbf u_B)]
$$

follows from those differing coordinates with the same Master Equation, Noether sea response, exposure definition, and observer projection. If a supposedly shared coordinate must also be changed privately, the proposed factorization has failed.

Plainly: compare close relatives. If most of the architecture is truly shared, their differences reveal which remaining geometric coordinate does the work.

| Controlled comparison | Rows held common | Difference that needs a native carrier | Architecture clue |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| electron, muon, and tau | electric charge, fermion transformation class, charged-lepton interaction grammar | mass/exposure hierarchy and destructive-corridor measure | generation cannot be only a new charge carrier; shielding, phase coherence, or basin access must vary on one family map |
| pion versus rho | quark--antiquark flavor inventory and color-singlet composite class | pseudoscalar spin-0 versus vector spin-1 angular and parity response, mass gap, and strong exit width | relative ordered-frame alignment or an internal corridor mode must change while constituent identities remain fixed |
| nucleon versus Delta | baryon color-singlet class and, for matched charge rows, nearly shared flavor inventory | spin-$\tfrac12$ versus spin-$\tfrac32$, mass gap, and stability/width | three-constituent frame alignment and exit topology must be distinct from color closure itself |
| charged versus neutral pion | nearby isospin family and pseudoscalar class | radically different accessible decay corridors and lifetimes | channel connectivity and conjugate pairing can dominate lifetime without implying a different internal recurrence |
| neutral-kaon propagation modes | shared neutral-kaon source sector | coherent mixing, two survival scales, and $CP$-sensitive transport | at least one relative phase/holonomy and one exit-exposure coordinate are needed; a torsion picture remains only one realization |
| proton versus neutron | baryon composite grammar and color closure | net charge, electric/magnetic spatial response, beta corridor, and stability | integrated charge exposure, internal signed segregation, and weak exit access must vary without opening free color |
| photon versus $Z$ | spin-1 comparison class and transverse response | no rest branch/two free channels versus effective mass, longitudinal response, and finite width | propagating phase carrier and metastable vector corridor cannot be distinguished by spin label alone |
| photon versus $W^\pm$ | spin-1 comparison class | neutral source-free propagation versus charged weak routing, longitudinal response, and product corridors | charge inventory, basin role, and exposure rank are independent of vector polarization class |
| electron versus neutrino | fermionic spinor/exchange target | charged stable localized branch versus neutral coherent weak phase carrier | spinor history does not determine charge exposure, support dimension, or propagation morphology |

These comparisons supply a disciplined route from spectroscopy to geometry. The pion--rho and nucleon--Delta rows do not prove literal spin alignment or a vibrating flux tube. They require some native coordinate that changes angular response, mass-facing exposure, and exit topology while leaving the declared inventory and composite grammar fixed. The first test is therefore the full motion, frame, exposure, and survival fingerprint of both branches—not a label assigned after observing their spin.

Plainly: particle spectroscopy becomes geometric evidence when the theory can change one part of a shared architecture and predict several linked differences at once. Merely naming the excited state after its observed spin does not explain it.

### Multiplet Orbits Constrain Which Coordinates May Change

The strongest catalog clues are often not individual particles but families whose effective labels transform together. The [Quantum Number Mapping](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/fermions/quantum-number-mapping.md) owner records the current charge, weak, color, generation, baryon/lepton, and spin interfaces. Those labels remain observer-level recovery targets here. A candidate geometry must recover not only each label but the pattern of rows that remain invariant when one family member is replaced by another.

For an effective comparison family $\mathcal F$ and a declared family action $g$, let $\mathcal U_g$ act on the complete candidate history. A successful inverse map requires

$$
\Pi_S(\mathcal U_g\Theta)
\simeq
\rho_S(g)\Pi_S(\Theta),
$$

for every sector projection that should transform, while each declared spectator projection satisfies

$$
\Pi_{\mathrm{spect}}(\mathcal U_g\Theta)
\simeq
\Pi_{\mathrm{spect}}(\Theta).
$$

The same law, branch-construction rule, quotient functional, and tolerances are used throughout the family. These are equivariance targets—meaning that the native transformation and observer relabeling agree—not substrate symmetry postulates.

| Effective family action | Rows that transform | Rows that must remain common or covariant | Geometry pressure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| color-basis change | internal color projection and corridor routing | mass-facing scalar response, electric charge, generation, weak family, retention class, and laboratory rotation covariance | color needs an internal carried basis or equivalent response fiber; it cannot be a bare laboratory axis or a mass-changing spatial deformation |
| weak-doublet transition | charge, $T_3$-facing exposure, products, and charged-corridor payload | family provenance, spectator identities, color representation, and one weak docking grammar | up/down partners need a common corridor interface plus an inventory-complete decoration change; label swapping alone is insufficient |
| isospin-relative comparison | electric exposure and small symmetry-breaking mass/response rows | leading strong-sector closure and association grammar | proton/neutron and pion-family relatives favor a shared confined core with a charge/exposure coordinate rather than unrelated hadron geometries |
| generation step | mass/shielding response, lifetime, mixing overlap, and some exit access | electric charge class, color representation, weak representation, spinor class, and common coupling grammar | generation is a branch-family coordinate over a common representation-bearing core, not a new charge or color carrier |
| excitation within one inventory | angular/parity response, native mode content, mass-facing ledger, and exit topology | constituent identities, total charge, color-singlet closure, and family labels | $\pi/\rho$ and nucleon/$\Delta$ require lawful frame, deformation, phase, or corridor modes of a shared composite grammar |
| whole-history conjugation | signed electric exposure, conjugate reaction corridors, and signed baryon/lepton provenance | scalar mass response, mode count, retention class, and covariantly conjugated color/weak representations | conjugation must act on the whole history and cannot be identified with spatial mirroring, circulation reversal, or a new constituent design |

Plainly: a multiplet tells us which knobs nature can turn independently. If a color change alters mass, a generation change alters charge, or conjugation requires rebuilding the whole object with unrelated rules, the proposed geometry has tied together coordinates that observations keep separate.

This gives a particularly sharp color test. On a candidate color orbit $\{\mathcal U_c\Theta:c\in\{1,2,3\}\}$, require one common scalar mass-facing response and electric projection within tolerance,

$$
\Delta_{\mathrm{color,inv}}
=
\max_{c,c'}
\left[
d_M(\Pi_M\mathcal U_c\Theta,\Pi_M\mathcal U_{c'}\Theta)
+
d_Q(\Pi_Q\mathcal U_c\Theta,\Pi_Q\mathcal U_{c'}\Theta)
\right]
\le
\epsilon_{\mathrm{color,inv}},
$$

while the color-facing projections remain distinct and compose into the declared singlet and reconfiguration rows. A literal exceptional spatial axis that produces orientation-dependent inertial or electric behavior fails even if its three names reproduce the color count.

The isospin-relative row has a complementary burden. The leading confined association/corridor record must be reusable while the electric and weak-facing decoration changes. Exact mass degeneracy is not required: electromagnetic, quark-mass-facing, environment, and detector rows remain explicit inputs to the observer comparison. The useful falsifier is stronger than a mass mismatch: a candidate fails the shared-core hypothesis if changing the charge partner also requires an unrelated color-closure graph or private strong response law.

### Independent Labels Require An Operation Algebra

Multiplet covariance also constrains the order in which complete-history operations may be applied. Let $\mathcal A_g$ and $\mathcal A_h$ be two native record actions and let $Q_{g,h}$ remove only the common declared gauge or observer redundancy. For operations whose effective actions are independent, define the commutator residual

$$
\Delta_{g,h}^{\mathrm{comm}}(\Theta)
=
d_{g,h}
\left(
Q_{g,h}\mathcal A_g\mathcal A_h\Theta,
Q_{g,h}\mathcal A_h\mathcal A_g\Theta
\right).
$$

The comparison passes only when $\Delta_{g,h}^{\mathrm{comm}}\le\epsilon_{g,h}^{\mathrm{comm}}$ with one frozen law, identity map, quotient, and tolerance. This does not assume that every observer operation commutes. A deliberately noncommuting transition must instead route the mismatch into a named payload, product, recoil, wake, sea, or boundary row:

$$
Q_{g,h}\mathcal A_g\mathcal A_h\Theta
\not\simeq
Q_{g,h}\mathcal A_h\mathcal A_g\Theta
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal R_{g,h}^{\mathrm{event}}\ne0.
$$

Plainly: if two labels are truly independent, changing their order should not quietly produce a different particle. If order matters, the theory must show the physical transaction that carries the difference.

| Operation pair | Required composition behavior | Candidate geometry pressure |
| --- | --- | --- |
| proper spatial rotation and internal color action | commute after the complete spatial record and internal frame are transformed consistently | color cannot be a fixed laboratory axis or an untransported mark on the drawing |
| generation step and electric-charge projection | generation action leaves the charge representation invariant | shielding depletion or phase change cannot delete or replace the charge carrier privately |
| generation step and color action | commute on the common representation domain | color exceptionality must remain available in every generation rather than collapse with a shielding tier |
| global polarity conjugation and spatial parity | remain independently composable on the primitive kernel domain | matter/antimatter, mirror orientation, and circulation cannot be one binary switch |
| color reconfiguration and strong-singlet composition | preserve the singlet quotient while routing corridor strain and recoil | a color change must be an internal graph action, not destruction and recreation of the hadron |
| weak-doublet transition and spectator identity | commute on every spectator subrecord while the active payload and products change | the corridor must localize its geometry surgery and preserve all nonparticipating identities and histories |
| physical rotation and composite exchange | give the same exchange class after common rotation of the full apparatus/history | exchange statistics cannot depend on an arbitrary laboratory orientation |

These rows produce an operation graph for candidate search. Its vertices are complete-history actions; a commute edge carries a residual tolerance, and a transaction edge carries a required event ledger. A candidate is rejected if an expected commute edge changes an unrelated observer label, or if a noncommuting edge has no physical mismatch record.

The operation graph is stronger than a list of quantum numbers. Two static labels can be assigned independently on paper while their proposed geometric actions interfere. For example, an exceptional laboratory axis can reproduce three color names until the object is rotated; a generation model can preserve charge labels until its depletion operation removes the sites that supposedly carry charge. The commutator residual tests the actual construction.

Claim grade: the composition obligations are deduced recovery requirements on the declared observer domains. The particular native actions and quotient are candidate-dependent. Falsifier: a candidate with a nonzero expected-commutator residual nevertheless reproduces every observer composition and ledger through one fixed projection, or a supposedly transaction-bearing noncommutator closes with no payload or provenance difference.

### Additive Quantum Labels Require Provenance Homomorphisms

Baryon and lepton labels cannot be reduced to electric charge, polarity count, or visual constituent number. A neutron has nonzero baryon label and zero net charge; a neutrino has nonzero lepton label and zero net charge; a meson has two quark-facing constituents but zero net baryon label. The minimum native carrier is therefore an association-class and reaction-provenance row.

On a declared observer domain where an additive label $q_X$ is conserved, its projection from accepted branch/composite records must satisfy

$$
q_X(\Theta_1\boxplus\Theta_2)
=
q_X(\Theta_1)+q_X(\Theta_2),
\qquad
q_X(\mathcal C\Theta)=-q_X(\Theta),
$$

and for an event $\alpha\to\beta$,

$$
\Delta q_X
=
\sum_{B\in\beta}q_X(B)
-
\sum_{A\in\alpha}q_X(A)
$$

must equal the explicitly classified source, remnant, sea, or violation row. Here $\boxplus$ is composition of independently identity-bearing records, not ordinary vector addition, and $\mathcal C$ is whole-history conjugation. This is a recovery grammar for additive observer labels, not a new primitive conservation law. A process outside the declared conservation domain requires a named nonzero event topology rather than silent ledger loss.

For a confined quark-facing record, a fractional baryon entry belongs to its role inside the composite association graph; it does not license a free asymptotic quark branch. Color singlet closure and baryon additivity are also different constraints: meson color closure pairs conjugate entries to net baryon zero, while baryon color closure combines three quark-facing entries to the integer observer label.

Plainly: baryon and lepton number are best treated as durable routing labels on whole reaction histories. They tell us how a branch is associated and where it came from, not how much electric charge it has or how many dots appear in a drawing.

### Quantized Charge Requires A Locally Constant Exposure Index

Effective electric charge has two roles that a geometry must not alias. The charge label is a discrete signed source/receiver and reaction-bookkeeping row; the electromagnetic response strength can depend on scale, environment, screening, and the derived source--sea constitutive map. A varying coupling is not a continuously changing charge quantum.

For an accepted charged branch family, define a representation-facing charge index

$$
\kappa_Q:
\mathcal R_Q/Q_Q
\longrightarrow
\Lambda_Q,
$$

where $\Lambda_Q$ is the observer charge lattice to be recovered and $Q_Q$ removes only declared redundancies. The normalized charge label is

$$
q_{\mathrm{label}}(\Theta)
=
q_0\,\kappa_Q(\Theta).
$$

The geometry requirements are:

1. $\kappa_Q$ is locally constant under admissible within-basin deformation, translation, rotation, weak electric polarization, and ordinary scale probing;
2. whole-history conjugation gives $\kappa_Q(\mathcal C\Theta)=-\kappa_Q(\Theta)$;
3. independently composed accepted records add their charge labels under the declared association rule;
4. a charge-changing event crosses a classified branch/corridor boundary and routes the exact index difference through products, remnants, source, wakes, sea, and boundary provenance; and
5. one universal $q_0$ serves leptons, quark-facing records, hadrons, weak charged products, and conjugates, even though their response quotients and spatial exposures differ.

Equivalently, for every admissible tangent perturbation $\delta\Theta\in T_\Theta\mathcal B_{\kappa_Q}$ inside one fixed charge basin,

$$
\left.D\kappa_Q\right|_{\Theta}(\delta\Theta)=0.
$$

The derivative statement is local and diagnostic. It does not require that the full electromagnetic response or measured form factor be constant.

Plainly: a charged object may stretch, polarize, move, or look different at another resolution without becoming fractionally more charged. Its charge label changes only through a real, ledgered transition to a different branch.

Several native carriers remain possible:

| Candidate charge carrier | What supplies discreteness | Decisive burden |
| --- | --- | --- |
| signed polarity inventory | an integer-valued signed identity sum with a common exposure normalization | prove the selected inventories are retained and reaction-complete; count alone does not establish site placement or exposure |
| topology or history winding | an integer index invariant under admissible deformation | derive conjugation, additivity, source response, and branch-changing events from the same history index |
| discrete exposure eigenclass | isolated eigenvalues or response basins of one retained morphology | show why intermediate exposures are not retained charge species and why polarization does not move the label |
| phase/permutation lock | a finite set of robust return classes with a charge-facing projection | distinguish charge sign/magnitude from circulation, spinor parity, and generation locks |
| composite association sum | additive composition of already quantized constituent-facing labels | recover integer hadron/ion charge while preserving confinement and avoiding free unaccepted fractional branches |

The current six-unit polarity decoration is one candidate in the first row. It would explain the common denominator only after ordinary dynamics selects the allowed charge classes, rejects unused signed arrangements, and supplies one universal observer normalization. Quantization alone does not establish six architrinos, six sites, axial placement, external exposure, or even an inventory realization.

Charge universality adds a cross-morphology test. Electron, muon, and tau branches must share the same $q_{\mathrm{label}}$ despite different mass/shielding and exit records. Proton charge must match the conjugate electron magnitude after composite projection despite a different internal association graph. Quark-facing fractional labels must sum correctly inside color-singlet composites without producing a free asymptotic fractional branch. One fitted normalization per morphology fails this intersection.

Claim grade: local constancy, conjugation, additivity, universal normalization, and complete charge-changing event routing are deduced recovery requirements. The five native carriers are inferred alternatives. Falsifier: an accepted fixed-law family continuously varies the conserved charge label inside one retained basin, requires morphology-specific charge units, or leaves an additional stable charge class with no observer counterpart.

Three new non-aliasing edges follow:

$$
Q\not\equiv(B,L),
\qquad
\text{color closure}\not\equiv B,
\qquad
\text{generation}\not\equiv(B,L).
$$

Their direct falsifiers are respectively a neutral neutron/neutrino collision in the first map, meson-versus-baryon closure in the second, and common baryon/lepton labels across generations in the third. A candidate may realize the rows through shared geometry, but it must not force them to be the same coordinate.

### Composite Spin Requires Frame And Exchange Composition

The [Mesons](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/mesons/mesons.md) owner uses alignment language for $\pi/\rho$ and nucleon/$\Delta$ comparisons, while the [Quarks](../../../content/markdown/aaa/assemblies/fermions/quarks.md) owner retains a hadronization spin-correlation burden. Those are useful shorthand targets, not evidence that constituent spin is a set of classical arrows.

For a composite candidate $H$ with constituent histories $\Theta_i$, retain the relative ordered frames

$$
R_{ij}(T)=F_i(T)^{\mathsf T}F_j(T),
$$

the relative support and motion history, color/association graph, corridor response, and wake/sea angular ledger. A minimum composite record is

$$
\Theta_H^{\mathrm{ang}}
=
\left(
\{\Theta_i,F_i\},
\{R_{ij}\},
\Gamma_{\mathrm{assoc}},
\mathcal J_{\mathrm{rel}},
\mathcal J_{\mathrm{corr}},
\mathcal J_{\mathrm{wake/sea}},
\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ex}}
\right).
$$

The observer angular-momentum class is a projection of this complete record. It is not determined by $\sum_i\boldsymbol\Omega_i$, by a count of clockwise constituents, or by one static dot product between axes. In particular, the full ledger must close schematically as

$$
\mathbf J_{\mathrm{obs}}
\longleftarrow
\mathcal P_J
\left[
\sum_i\mathcal J_i
+\mathcal J_{\mathrm{rel}}
+\mathcal J_{\mathrm{corr}}
+\mathcal J_{\mathrm{wake/sea}}
\right],
$$

where the arrow denotes an observer recovery map rather than an architrino-level vector-addition premise.

Plainly: the total spin label belongs to how several complete moving histories fit together. Internal frames, motion around one another, the binding corridor, and the sea can all carry angular information, so “parallel” is not yet a calculation.

Where an exchange-sector factorization is declared, an identical-constituent exchange path $p$ supplies comparison characters

$$
\chi_{\mathrm{tot}}(p)
=
\chi_{\mathrm{supp}}(p)
\chi_{\mathrm{frame}}(p)
\chi_{\mathrm{flavor}}(p)
\chi_{\mathrm{color}}(p)
\chi_{\mathrm{corr}}(p),
$$

with the product required to recover the complete fermionic exchange class. Each factor must be measured from the corresponding history quotient; none may be inserted as a sign label. If the subrecords do not factor, the owner must evaluate one joint exchange holonomy instead of forcing this product form.

This creates four controlled composite tests:

| Comparison | Rows held fixed | Row that must change | Geometry inference |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $\pi$ versus $\rho$ | quark--antiquark identities, flavor, charge, and color-singlet association class | pseudoscalar spin-$0$ versus vector spin-$1$ response, mass-facing ledger, and strong exit | relative frames, corridor mode, and possibly support motion must expose different response ranks without changing inventory |
| nucleon versus $\Delta$ | three-quark color-singlet grammar and matched flavor rows where applicable | spin-$\tfrac12$ versus spin-$\tfrac32$, mass-facing response, and exit measure | a composite frame/corridor mode must change while color closure remains valid |
| $\Delta^{++}$ identical-quark exchange | three same-flavor quark-facing records and one color-singlet composite | total exchange class under each identical-constituent transposition | color association, frame, support, and corridor histories must compose to the required exchange sign; three different color names alone are insufficient |
| proton versus neutron | spin-$\tfrac12$ composite class and leading color closure | electric/weak-facing decoration and beta corridor | charge change must not rebuild the spin/exchange or color-closure architecture |

Plainly: close relatives let us change one composite coordinate at a time. The same pair or triplet must support different angular classes, while the $\Delta^{++}$ asks whether color and exchange geometry jointly prevent three identical quark-facing histories from collapsing into an inconsistent state.

The strongest immediate rejection is an `alignment-only` hadron model. If its only internal coordinate is a binary parallel/antiparallel label, it cannot simultaneously derive continuous rotation covariance, half-integer versus integer composite representations, parity, exchange composition, orbital or corridor contribution, polarization-dependent production, and decay angular correlations. The smallest repair is not necessarily another constituent; it is a complete relative-frame and exchange-history record whose response rank is measured under ordinary evolution.

Claim grade: the need for distinct complete-history angular and exchange rows is deduced from the controlled recovery targets. Their factorization into the five $\chi$ rows and any literal frame/corridor realization are inferred diagnostics. Falsifier: one alignment-only fixed-law candidate, with no hidden history or response coordinate, recovers all four controlled comparisons and their rotation, parity, exchange, production, and decay records.

### Conjugation, Orientation, And Spinor History Must Be Separate

The declared polarity-product radial kernel supplies an exact separation that rules out a large class of one-sign braid pictures. Let $C$ globally conjugate polarity at fixed worldlines and let $P$ mirror the worldlines at fixed polarity labels. On the declared B1 sign chart, let $c_{\mathrm{pol}}$ be the polarity-assignment sign, $o_{\mathrm{PA}}$ the ordered geometric orientation, and $\chi$ the polarity-weighted handedness. Then

$$
\chi=o_{\mathrm{PA}}c_{\mathrm{pol}},
$$

with transformation rows

$$
C:
(c_{\mathrm{pol}},o_{\mathrm{PA}},\chi)
\mapsto
(-c_{\mathrm{pol}},o_{\mathrm{PA}},-\chi),
$$

$$
P:
(c_{\mathrm{pol}},o_{\mathrm{PA}},\chi)
\mapsto
(c_{\mathrm{pol}},-o_{\mathrm{PA}},-\chi).
$$

The full kernel is covariant under both operations and $CP$ preserves $\chi$. These rows are exact sign bookkeeping for the declared kernel; they do not establish branch formation, retention, or weak-channel $CP$ behavior.

Now let $\eta_{2\pi}\in\mathbb Z_2$ be the quotient-surviving sheet parity of a physical rotation loop. Neither $C$ nor $P$ determines this row. Fermion spinor support instead requires

$$
\eta_{2\pi}=1,
\qquad
\eta_{4\pi}=0,
$$

on a retained non-gauge history with doubled-path restoration and closed angular-momentum ledger. Therefore one circulation sign cannot encode all of matter/antimatter conjugation, left/right geometric orientation, and $2\pi/4\pi$ spinor history: the three signs answer different transformation tests.

The immediate geometry consequence is conditional but strong. A fermion candidate must carry a framed, history-lifted retained record rather than rely on a spinning-loop picture alone. Any candidate that uses circulation or polarity-weighted handedness must keep those coordinates distinct from conjugation, parity, and spinor-sheet history; its causal roots, angular-momentum ledger, and sector exposure must remain inspectable on the same record.

Plainly: electron versus positron, a geometry and its mirror, clockwise versus counterclockwise motion, and fermionic $4\pi$ restoration are not four names for one switch. A viable braid must carry enough structure to distinguish them.

### Same-Record Orientation Experiment

The highest-discrimination next test for any evolved candidate branch is to construct, without changing its constitutive law:

1. global polarity conjugation $C$ at fixed worldline order;
2. spatial mirroring $P$ at fixed polarity assignment;
3. circulation reversal with support and inventory fixed;
4. physical $2\pi$ and $4\pi$ orientation transports; and
5. a two-assembly exchange path when two retained copies exist.

For every continuation, record active roots, emission order, causal writhe, framing, polarity assignment, ordered orientation, total assembly--wake--sea angular momentum, return residual, exposure, and branch stability. A visible $2\pi$ return with every retained non-gauge row identical is an ordinary $SO(3)$ return, not spinor support. A failed $4\pi$ restoration is a branch reconfiguration or broken return, not evidence for a spinor lift.

Plainly: use one evolved object and change one geometric operation at a time. That experiment can tell us which signs are genuinely independent before we try to read charge, moment, weak handedness, or exchange statistics from them.

### Complete-Record Symmetry Action

A static picture is not symmetric in the physically relevant sense unless the symmetry acts on the complete causal-history record. Let

$$
\Theta_W
=
\left(
\{q_a,\mathbf X_a(T),\mathbf V_a(T)\}_a,
\mathcal R_W,
\mathcal F_{\mathrm{ord}},
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{ax}},
\mathcal E_{\mathrm{weak}},
\mathcal C_{\mathrm{col}},
\mathcal N_{\mathrm{sea}},
\mathcal P_{\mathrm{prov}}
\right),
$$

where the added rows contain roots and wakes, ordered frame and phase, indexed axis data, weak exposure, color comparison data, sea state, persistent identities, and reaction provenance. For each effective symmetry target $g$, the minimum candidate certificate has a native record action

$$
\mathcal A_g:\Theta_W\longrightarrow\Theta_W^{(g)},
$$

with observer projection

$$
\Pi_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathcal A_g\Theta_W)
\simeq
\rho_g\Pi_{\mathrm{obs}}(\Theta_W).
$$

A comparison residual must include both observer covariance and ledger covariance, together with an explicit no-retuning row:

$$
\Delta_g
=
d_{\mathrm{obs}}
\left(
\Pi_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathcal A_g\Theta_W),
\rho_g\Pi_{\mathrm{obs}}(\Theta_W)
\right)
+
d_{\mathrm{led}}
\left(
L(\mathcal A_g\Theta_W),
\mathcal A_gL(\Theta_W)
\right)
+P_{\mathrm{retune}}.
$$

Here $L$ is the frozen ledger projection that returns persistent identities, polarity, action, energy, momentum, angular momentum, wake, sea, and reaction provenance rows. $P_{\mathrm{retune}}\ge0$ is a declared penalty functional of the two compared records and is zero only when the law, coefficients, initial record construction, quotient, and observer projection are unchanged except for the action $\mathcal A_g$. Before evaluation, the owner freezes component tolerances $\epsilon_{g,\mathrm{obs}}$ and $\epsilon_{g,\mathrm{led}}$ and a retuning null $P_{\mathrm{retune}}=0$; acceptance requires both component distances to lie within their tolerances rather than relying on cancellation in one scalar sum.

When transformations compose, one declared quotient $Q$ must also satisfy

$$
Q\mathcal A_g\mathcal A_h\Theta_W
\simeq
Q\mathcal A_{gh}\Theta_W.
$$

$Q$ may remove common translation, time origin, or another explicitly nonphysical coordinate. It may not erase persistent identity, causal-root branch, chirality, exchange order, or a proposed $2\pi$ sheet change merely to make the comparison pass.

Plainly: a symmetry test has to transform the entire causal record and then compare both what an observer sees and whether every identity and ledger row still closes. A hidden parameter change or a quotient that deletes the hard part is a failed test.

Plainly: rotate, mirror, conjugate, or transport the entire history—not only the final drawing. The same rules and parameters must then produce the familiar observer transformation.

| Effective symmetry clue | Weakest native action | Key geometry distinction and falsifier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| proper spatial rotation | rotate positions, velocities, roots/wakes, sea state, and ordered frames together | physical rotation must not change internal color unless a color-corridor event occurs |
| $2\pi/4\pi$ return | lift a visible frame rotation to an ordered-history action with a non-gauge $\mathbb Z_2$ sheet row | identical complete return at $2\pi$ rejects elementary spinor support; failure to restore at $4\pi$ also rejects it |
| $C$ | conjugate every polarity and the corresponding source/receiver ledgers at fixed spatial order | antimatter is not merely a mirror; pro/anti ordered orientation must remain independently recorded |
| $P$ | mirror the entire spatial and ordered-frame record while distinguishing polar and axial rows | particle parity and weak parity violation do not follow from visual handedness alone |
| effective $T$ comparison | reverse effective motion, cycle, and phase-flow labels through an admissible forward-causal comparison record | it must not reverse absolute time or introduce advanced causal roots |
| effective $CPT$ comparison | compose the three effective record actions on one quotient/provenance domain | incompatible quotients or an inadmissible composed record fail the comparison; no substrate CPT premise follows |
| Lorentz recovery | continue one rest branch into one drifted branch and export clocks, rulers, signals, shape, roots, stability, and sector responses | passing one optical cancellation while matter, color, or weak rows need private coefficients fails common-mode recovery |
| color triplet/octet/singlet | act on a persistent internal exceptionality space; realize triplet states, composable traceless reconfiguration modes, and singlet invariance | laboratory $x/y/z$ orientation is not color; eight matrices do not establish eight physical modes |
| weak doublet and chirality | expose two weak-visible branches over one retained core through one handed corridor grammar | polarity count or a static mirror cannot replace spinor history and handed exposure |
| symmetry breaking | change one branch order parameter so the effective channel stabilizer and response Hessian change | separately tuned photon, $W$, and $Z$ gaps or extra uncontrolled channels reject the shared branch variable |

The response representation itself has an exact Euclidean decomposition. For any same-record spatial response $K_{ij}$,

$$
K^{(0)}_{ij}
=
\frac{\operatorname{tr}K}{3}\delta_{ij},
$$

$$
K^{(1)}_{ij}
=
\frac{K_{ij}-K_{ji}}{2},
$$

$$
K^{(2)}_{ij}
=
\frac{K_{ij}+K_{ji}}{2}
-
\frac{\operatorname{tr}K}{3}\delta_{ij}.
$$

Under a proper rotation each part transforms as $K^{(r)}\mapsto RK^{(r)}R^{\mathsf T}$. A scalar candidate suppresses the antisymmetric and symmetric trace-free pieces. In three dimensions the antisymmetric rank-two piece is dual to an axial-vector response; a general polar-vector channel requires a separately defined rank-one response. A tensor candidate retains a symmetric trace-free response while passing its separate propagation and leakage tests. This classification comes from the evolved response, not the particle name.

Plainly: the antisymmetric part describes an oriented plane, equivalently an axial direction in three dimensions. It must not be mistaken for every kind of ordinary directed response.

At effective grade, one shared electroweak branch/order-parameter candidate would have to supply a response Hessian $H_{\mathrm{EW}}$ and a positive kinetic/response metric $G_{\mathrm{kin}}$ on one canonically declared coordinate domain. Its generalized eigenproblem

$$
H_{\mathrm{EW}}v
=
\lambda G_{\mathrm{kin}}v
$$

must produce the spectrum pattern

$$
\operatorname{spec}_{G_{\mathrm{kin}}}H_{\mathrm{EW}}(\Theta_W)
=
\{0,m_W^2,m_W^2,m_Z^2\}
$$

within a frozen spectrum residual $\Delta_{\mathrm{EW}}$ and owner-declared tolerance $\epsilon_{\mathrm{EW}}$. This is a recovery target and does not import a primitive gauge symmetry or substrate Higgs mechanism. Without a declared $G_{\mathrm{kin}}$, the raw coordinate Hessian eigenvalues carry no invariant mass interpretation.

Plainly: the same response matrix can be separated into directionless, rotational, and shape-changing parts. That gives a native way to test scalar, vector, and tensor behavior without assuming what the candidate is supposed to be.

#### Boson Response-Rank Grammar

The scalar/vector/tensor decomposition does not by itself count propagating or transactional modes. For one fixed evolved sector record $\Theta_s$ and declared event window $W$, define the response Gram operator

$$
G_{ab}^{(s)}(\Theta_s;W)
=
\left\langle
\mathcal R_a^{(s)}[\Theta_s],
\mathcal R_b^{(s)}[\Theta_s]
\right\rangle_W.
$$

For a declared propagation or interaction axis $\hat{\mathbf k}$, let

$$
P_\perp=I-\hat{\mathbf k}\hat{\mathbf k}^{\mathsf T},
\qquad
P_\parallel=\hat{\mathbf k}\hat{\mathbf k}^{\mathsf T},
$$

and measure resolved ranks at a frozen threshold $\tau$,

$$
n_T^{(s)}
=
\operatorname{rank}_\tau(P_\perp G^{(s)}P_\perp),
\qquad
n_L^{(s)}
=
\operatorname{rank}_\tau(P_\parallel G^{(s)}P_\parallel).
$$

These ranks are extracted from native evolution and one observer projection; they are not polarization states inserted into the Master Equation. The effective recovery limits are two transverse and no free longitudinal response for photon and perturbative-gluon charts, while a fully recovered massive vector chart must expose an additional longitudinal response. The current corpus owns only the weaker $W/Z$ requirement that longitudinal or mixed-axis response be allowed; an explicit exactly-three-mode benchmark is absent.

Plainly: count independent ways the evolved history can respond across and along its direction of motion. Do not count strands in a drawing or assign polarization labels before the response exists.

The least-committal comparison architecture is

$$
\Theta_{\mathrm{vector}}
=
\left(
\text{axis},
\text{transverse two-plane},
\text{relative phase},
\mathbf J\text{ ledger},
\text{wake/sea provenance}
\right),
$$

with sector extensions:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Theta_\gamma
&=
(\Theta_{\mathrm{vector}},
\text{neutral singlet},
\text{null/no-rest closure}),\\
\Theta_g
&=
(\Theta_{\mathrm{vector}},
\text{color action},
\text{tube provenance},
\text{confinement/self-coupling}),\\
\Theta_{W/Z}
&=
(\Theta_{\mathrm{vector}},
\text{localization/stiffness},
\text{longitudinal response},
\text{weak payload}).
\end{aligned}
$$

This is a proposed comparison hierarchy, not a common retained boson. Equal local mode count cannot identify photon and gluon, while a planar two-mode carrier cannot serve $W/Z$ without a derived localization/longitudinal extension. A tube or corridor axis without transverse phase, angular-momentum, recoil, and sea ledgers remains only an oriented shape.

A scalar candidate instead requires a rotational scalar response (tensor rank $0$, not matrix rank $0$), with vector and higher-multipole leakage below tolerance on the same preparation, production, decay, and detector record. Internally anisotropic geometry can project to a scalar by cancellation, and a visibly radial motion can still leak vector or tensor response. Scalarity therefore implies neither spherical microscopic geometry nor positive parity; scalar, pseudoscalar, and mixed-$CP$ cases require separate whole-history $P/CP$ actions and decay-plane comparisons.

One especially sharp missing angular null is the effective spin-1 exclusion for two identical on-shell transverse photon products, with off-shell, nonidentical-vector, and scalar-parent controls. The corpus mentions the diphoton channel as evidence against spin 1 but has no explicit owner or same-record residual for this constraint. It should remain a coverage gap until a boson carrier can supply exchange, angular-ledger, and product-basin records; a generic inability to produce two photons would not constitute the required symmetry cancellation.

Plainly: boson families may reuse an oriented phase grammar, but photon, gluon, weak-vector, and scalar roles demand different additional information. Those differences are candidate eliminators, not decorative particle labels.

### Relational Geometry, Exchange, And Measurement

Several of the strongest Standard Model and quantum constraints do not act on one isolated braid. They act on the complete history of two or more retained assemblies or propagating carrier histories, their common source or formation event, the intervening sea, and the apparatuses that eventually produce records. The minimum relational record is

$$
\Theta_{AB}^{\mathrm{rel}}
=
\left(
\Theta_A,\Theta_B,
\Gamma_{AB}^{\mathrm{src}},
\Gamma_{AB}^{\mathrm{path}},
\mathcal N_{\mathrm{sea}}^{AB},
\Theta_{\mathrm{app},A},
\Theta_{\mathrm{app},B},
\mathcal P_{AB}
\right),
$$

where $\Gamma_{AB}^{\mathrm{src}}$ records common creation provenance, $\Gamma_{AB}^{\mathrm{path}}$ records later identity-preserving exchange or separation history, and $\mathcal P_{AB}$ is the joint observer projection. This record must not be replaced by two isolated particle snapshots plus a correlation label.

Plainly: some clues concern the relationship between two objects, not hidden features inside either object by itself. Their shared history can be physical information even after the objects are far apart.

Four logically different burdens then constrain geometry:

1. **Exchange.** Two identical assemblies must be transported through a lawful two-assembly history while persistent substrate identities remain tracked. The observer quotient may exchange the effective slots, but it may not erase the path history before the exchange class is measured. The fermionic sign must come from the same retained non-gauge ordered-frame row that supplies the $2\pi/4\pi$ return, not from a separately assigned statistics label.
2. **Apparatus resolution.** A Stern--Gerlach-like record is a finite-time assembly--apparatus interaction with two stable outcome basins. The apparatus axis couples to the full ordered-frame, wake, sea, exposure, and angular-ledger history; it does not read a pre-existing tiny arrow. A viable fermion morphology must therefore admit a continuous orientation response whose pushed-forward record measure produces two discrete basins and the required angle dependence.
3. **Pair correlations.** A Bell-pair candidate must preserve measurement independence and no-signaling while its completed joint response remains nonfactorizable. A common source label is insufficient if it screens the later records into two independent local response laws.
4. **Measurement context.** One substrate history must support different apparatus-defined record maps without assigning a simultaneous outcome to every possible apparatus context. The assembly carries the coordinates that respond to an apparatus; it need not carry a global table of already formed records for apparatuses that were not present.

Exchange gives an exact endpoint-snapshot no-go. Let $\widetilde\Gamma_{12}$ be a lawful exchange history and let $Q_{\mathrm{obs}}$ remove only provenance distinctions unavailable to the declared apparatus. An exchange classifier has the form

$$
\chi_{\mathrm{ex}}:
[Q_{\mathrm{obs}}\widetilde\Gamma_{12}]
\longrightarrow U(1).
$$

If the classifier instead depends only on the final unordered snapshot, a one-exchange history, a double exchange, and a no-exchange control that return to the same snapshot receive the same value. Such a classifier cannot recover a nontrivial fermionic sign or anyonic phase. The minimal comparison must include no exchange, one exchange, reversed exchange, and double exchange on the same retained branches, sea state, apparatus, and quotient.

Plainly: the route by which two identical objects trade places matters even when the ending picture is unchanged. Statistics therefore constrains the history carried by the geometry, not just its final shape.

For the third burden, let $x$ and $y$ denote the two apparatus settings and $\Pi$ the complete retained pair provenance. The candidate is inside the excluded product-screened class if

$$
P(a,b\mid x,y)
=
\int
P_A(a\mid x,\Pi)
P_B(b\mid y,\Pi)
\,d\rho_{\mathrm{src}}(\Pi).
$$

Consequently, two independent detector-facing braid responses plus any setting-independent common-past label cannot close the observed joint law. The missing native coordinate must reside in a derived non-product joint response, a non-restartable relational history, or another explicitly named structure that is not algebraically equivalent to the product form. It must do so without inserting later settings into the source record and without opening a controllable signal between the wings.

Plainly: merely saying that the particles were created together does not solve the correlation problem. If their later responses can still be calculated as two independent local responses averaged over that shared past, the candidate has failed the decisive test.

Contextuality adds another exact obstruction. In a Mermin--Peres-style comparison, the six calibrated context products have signs $\{+1,+1,+1,+1,+1,-1\}$. A single context-independent assignment to every effective observable makes the product of all six rows $+1$, whereas the required context products multiply to $-1$. The native response must therefore be a family of apparatus-indexed record maps from one substrate dynamics, with compatible shared marginals, rather than a geometry carrying a global value for every unperformed measurement.

Plainly: the apparatus does more than reveal a hidden answer. Its geometry is part of the physical process that creates the durable record, although the same underlying dynamics must remain consistent across apparatus choices.

This yields high-discrimination geometry intersections:

| Intersection | Native requirement | Candidate family eliminated |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $2\pi/4\pi$ return $\times$ fermionic exchange | one non-gauge ordered-history row must control both single-assembly rotation and two-assembly exchange composition | candidates that assign spinor and exchange signs independently, or derive exclusion only from volume overlap |
| volumetric packing $\times$ helium-like shared spatial mode | exclusion must act on the complete spatial-plus-ordered-frame row, not on spatial overlap alone | universal hard-envelope pictures that forbid opposite-frame fermions from sharing one spatial mode |
| planar support $\times$ anyonic possibilities | two-dimensional support must carry a measured exchange holonomy; planarity alone cannot select Bose symmetry | every rule equating flattening with bosonic behavior |
| composite boson $\times$ retained constituent identity | whole-composite exchange must compose matched constituent exchange rows while preserving binding and the internal state | rules requiring every boson to be planar or every three-dimensional object to be fermionic |
| Stern--Gerlach two-basin record $\times$ continuous apparatus rotation | one orientation/history coordinate must couple covariantly to the apparatus and push forward into two outcomes with the correct angle law | preassigned binary spin labels with no assembly--apparatus dynamics, and classical fixed-arrow threshold models |
| contextual record products $\times$ shared marginals | context-indexed apparatus couplings must generate the required record algebra without a global preassigned value table | morphologies that store one simultaneous classical answer for every incompatible apparatus context |
| Bell correlation $\times$ measurement independence $\times$ no-signaling | retain a non-product relational response or non-restartable provenance structure whose one-wing marginals stay setting-independent | independent local braid responses screened by any common-past variable, later-setting contamination of the source, and direct measurement-time signaling |
| Bell record $\times$ Lorentz recovery | joint statistics, timing, analyzer calibration, and coincidence selection must not reveal the absolute frame | candidates that obtain the correlation only through preferred-frame detector drift or ordering leakage |

The relational clue is architectural: the candidate ontology cannot consist only of isolated retained objects whose full physical state is restartable from separate finite-time snapshots. At least some multi-assembly histories must carry physically consequential relation data through the source, path, and apparatus record. This does not yet identify whether that data is stored primarily in daughter braids, their unresolved wakes, the Noether sea, or a larger coupled history. Those are alternative realization families to be tested, not interchangeable descriptions.

The live corpus does not yet state one consistent Bell causal route. Some passages place the nonfactorizable response in a live finite-$c_f$ coordination channel during measurement, while other owners forbid detector-to-detector wake transfer outside causal reach and place the burden in local apparatus interactions plus non-restartable pair provenance. Before this clue can select a braid or sea geometry, a same-record test must declare whether the two measurement windows are within causal reach and identify exactly which factorization premise fails. The product-screening no-go is firm; the native carrier of the required non-product response remains unsettled.

Plainly: the single-particle braid is not the whole search target. The theory also needs a geometry of relationships—exchange paths, shared provenance, and apparatus-coupled history—that cannot always be cut into independent object states without losing observable information.

### Discrete Morphology Coordinates Already Present In The Corpus

The general record $\mathcal M_A$ has a useful discrete candidate subrecord:

$$
\mathfrak M_{\mathrm{disc}}(A)
=
\left(
N_{\mathrm{sc}},
n_+^{\mathrm{ax}},n_-^{\mathrm{ax}};
\mathbf s_{\mathrm{sh}},
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{ax}},
\eta_{\mathrm{exc}};
\mathcal P,
\Gamma_{\mathrm{corr}}
\right).
$$

Here $N_{\mathrm{sc}}$ is coherent neutral-scaffold inventory, $n_\pm^{\mathrm{ax}}$ are candidate charge-carrier polarity counts, $\mathbf s_{\mathrm{sh}}$ is the shielding-support vector, $\mathcal D_{\mathrm{ax}}$ lists the three ordered axial dyad classes, $\eta_{\mathrm{exc}}$ identifies an exceptional axis or `none`, $\mathcal P$ records planar versus volumetric support and longitudinal thickness, and $\Gamma_{\mathrm{corr}}$ is the labeled component/corridor graph.

Within the current six-site charged-fermion candidate catalog, and only within that catalog, the bookkeeping identities are

$$
\frac{Q(A)}{e}
=
\frac{n_+^{\mathrm{ax}}-n_-^{\mathrm{ax}}}{6},
\qquad
N_{\mathrm{sc}}
=
2\lVert\mathbf s_{\mathrm{sh}}\rVert_1,
\qquad
g
=
4-\lVert\mathbf s_{\mathrm{sh}}\rVert_1.
$$

The current candidate support vectors are $(1,1,1)$, $(1,1,0)$, and $(1,0,0)$ for Generations I, II, and III. The charged-lepton axial inventories are $(0,6)$ for the electron family and $(6,0)$ for its conjugate. Up-type and down-type quark rows use $(5,1)$ and $(2,4)$ respectively. These are exact combinatorial consequences of the declared candidate catalog, not evidence that the catalog's axial sites or depleted scaffolds are retained.

This exposes two especially sharp selection problems:

- the down-type $(2,4)$ inventory admits two distinct dyad partitions, so a dynamical rule must select one physical family rather than silently predict an extra down-like branch; and
- neutrino `3+3` language is weak-exposure bookkeeping in the current owner, not a literal six-site neutral axial inventory.

Plainly: counts and axis partitions already eliminate many pictures, but they are a search chart, not a discovered particle. The dynamics still has to say which counted configurations exist and remain assembled.

### Reaction As Geometry Surgery

A particle geometry is constrained not only by how it persists but by how it can be assembled, transformed, and disassembled. Represent an event as a labeled graph transformation

$$
\mathscr S_{\mathsf e}:
\left(
G_{\mathrm{in}},
\mathcal I_{\mathrm{pool}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{sea}}
\right)
\longrightarrow
\left(
G_{\mathrm{out}},
\mathcal I_{\mathrm{rem}},
\Theta'_{\mathrm{sea}}
\right),
$$

subject to identity-inventory conservation,

$$
\mathcal I(G_{\mathrm{in}})\sqcup\mathcal I_{\mathrm{pool}}
=
\mathcal I(G_{\mathrm{out}})\sqcup\mathcal I_{\mathrm{rem}},
$$

with an explicit provenance-preserving bijection between the incoming pool and outgoing or remnant identities. In particular, observer-level energy or a field label cannot by itself become an electron--positron pair. A pair event must identify the incoming, sea-recruited, or previously associated architrino inventory that is reorganized into the two conjugate product basins and any remnant. Net charge cancellation without this identity map is insufficient.

The transformation also requires valid causal provenance for every used root and edge, complete action, energy, momentum, angular-momentum, wake, sea, recoil, remnant, and boundary ledgers, and certified product-basin membership for every claimed retained product.

The smallest useful candidate move set is association/dissociation, phase or shielding relock, exceptional-axis rerouting, active weak-triad routing, planar-mode release/capture, conjugate-pair capture, and continuing backreaction. These names classify transformations; they are not new primitive laws. Every move must be realized by ordinary Master-Equation evolution or a classified root, collision, fold, escape, capture, or reaction boundary.

Plainly: a proposed braid is more credible when the same geometry explains how it is made and how it decays. Reactions are therefore structural probes of the braid, not merely arrows between particle names.

### Scattering Boundaries As Event-Geometry Clues

Observer-level scattering amplitudes provide a compact map of which transient channels exist and how complete events join. They remain recovery targets; an amplitude, propagator, pole, or gauge connection is not a primitive substrate object. Their inverse value is that each genuine boundary feature places a specific burden on the native transition graph.

Near an effective channel pole, the comparison amplitude factorizes as

$$
\mathcal A_n
\longrightarrow
\sum_h
\mathcal A_L^{(h)}
\frac{i}{P_I^2-m_h^2+i0}
\mathcal A_R^{(h)}
+
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{reg}}.
$$

The native interpretation to be tested is not a literal virtual particle. It is a cut of one event history through an admissible intermediate basin or metastable corridor $B_h$: the left history reaches $B_h$, the intermediate record carries the required inventory and provenance, and the right history leaves it for the final products. The residue must be reconstructed from those two history families and the same intermediate-state measure. Inserting the pole into an effective fit without a corresponding native channel does not constrain braid geometry.

Plainly: a real scattering resonance says that the reaction can temporarily organize itself in a repeatable intermediate way. The location and width of the peak constrain that temporary geometry and its exit routes, not necessarily a permanently retained particle.

This gives five event-geometry tests:

| Effective scattering clue | Native geometry requirement | Exact non-inference | Failure condition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| stable-state pole | a positive-width retained basin with the exported identity and admissible source/capture routes | the pole does not identify a visual knot or constituent count | no retained basin exists, or the fitted pole has no complete inventory-bearing history |
| resonance pole and width | a formed metastable assembly or transaction corridor plus classified rupture/first-exit histories whose measure produces the width | the width is not automatically an orbital frequency or assembly size | the peak is fitted while no formed carrier/corridor, classified exit or rupture surface, or product ledger exists |
| pole factorization | the same intermediate record must be reachable from the left event and consumable by the right event, with matching identity and exposure quotients | the comparison propagator is not a substrate worldline | separate left/right fits use incompatible intermediate records or normalizations |
| optical/total-channel closure | elastic loss and all inelastic product basins must share one source measure and one complete outgoing ledger | unitarity is not a primitive probability law for one architrino | apparent elastic agreement hides missing, duplicated, or unproven product channels |
| absence of spurious singularities | chart boundaries and alternative corridor decompositions that do not represent physical basins must cancel in the observer projection | cancellation does not prove a unique positive geometry | a candidate path parameter produces an observer-visible pole with no physical intermediate basin |

Amplitude zeros and strongly suppressed channels are equally useful. They can arise from symmetry, destructive phase relations among several admissible histories, missing exposure, or a disconnected transition graph. These mechanisms must not be collapsed. A geometry claim gains force when the same ordered paths that produce a neighboring allowed channel cancel or close for the null channel without changing the law.

A further comparison target asks whether color exceptionality and kinematic causal-root numerators are two projections of one branch geometry. If three effective color rows obey an oriented cancellation while their corresponding kinematic rows require unrelated fitted numerators, the proposed common motif has failed. Conversely, algebraic cancellation alone does not establish the physical color modes; the intermediate histories, singlet closure, and confinement rows remain required.

Plainly: scattering can expose the grammar of braid surgery. Real boundaries must correspond to real native routes, false chart boundaries must disappear, and several descriptions of the same intermediate event must agree on one provenance-bearing geometry.

### Pair Formation Constrains Precursor And Product Geometry

Electron--positron formation is unusually discriminating because the same event must join photon or strong-background geometry to retained conjugate fermion geometry. At observer grade, the two-photon threshold depends on

$$
s
=
2E_1E_2(1-\cos\theta)
\ge
4m_e^2c_\gamma^4.
$$

The formation decision therefore cannot depend only on $E_1+E_2$ or a local scalar energy density. At fixed energies, collinear packets fail to open the channel while counterpropagating packets maximize the available invariant. The native event record must retain both propagation axes, relative angle, packet overlap, timing, and polarization/helicity histories until the product basins are selected.

Plainly: two energetic packets do not form a pair merely because they occupy the same region. Their directions and complete overlap history matter.

The live effective cross-section owner also gives the near-threshold behavior

$$
\sigma_{\gamma\gamma}
\sim
\pi r_e^2
\left(
\beta+\beta^3+O(\beta^5)
\right),
\qquad
\beta
=
\sqrt{1-\frac{4m_e^2c_\gamma^4}{s}}.
$$

Thus the projected event measure opens continuously, approximately linearly in the outgoing product speed at threshold. This does not identify a native partial wave, barrier codimension, or basin volume: flux, phase space, capture measure, and detector projection may all contribute. It does reject a universal hard switch from zero to a fixed nonzero pair yield.

The minimum pair record is

$$
\Theta_{\mathrm{pair}}
=
\left(
\Theta_{\gamma_1},
\Theta_{\gamma_2},
\Theta_{\mathrm{pool}},
\Pi_{\mathsf e},
\mathcal B_{e^-},
\mathcal B_{e^+},
\mathcal L_{E\mathbf p\mathbf J},
\Theta'_{\mathrm{sea}},
\Theta_{\mathrm{rem}}
\right),
$$

with a provenance-preserving identity map

$$
\Pi_{\mathsf e}:
\mathcal I_{\gamma_1}
\sqcup
\mathcal I_{\gamma_2}
\sqcup
\mathcal I_{\mathrm{pool}}
\longrightarrow
\mathcal I_{e^-}
\sqcup
\mathcal I_{e^+}
\sqcup
\mathcal I_{\mathrm{rem}}.
$$

The two products must enter separately retained charge-conjugate basins, have opposite charge-facing exposure and conjugation-even mass-facing response, carry the required fermionic ordered-frame/exchange rows, and continue their motion, wakes, recoil, sea response, and backreaction after formation. The incoming photon records must terminate or change role explicitly. Count and net-charge closure without this identity and basin map is insufficient.

Plainly: pair production is a complete morphology reorganization, not a field label turning into two charges. The event must say where every architrino came from and demonstrate that both new assemblies actually persist.

One event grammar must distinguish three precursor topologies:

| Precursor | Distinct native burden |
| --- | --- |
| two-photon Breit--Wheeler-like route | two packet axes, overlap, relative angle, polarization, and mutual momentum closure |
| material-assisted one-photon route | one photon plus a momentum-balancing material/nuclear branch and its recoil/remnant history |
| slowly varying strong-background route | continued source--sea history whose rare capture measure has the required exponential weak-tail behavior |

The product basins may be common while the approach corridors differ. A photon-energy-only switch cannot recover the first two, and one universal hard threshold cannot recover both the kinematic two-photon onset and the strong-background tail.

At threshold, the incoming photons may carry nonzero angular and polarization records even as the product relative speed tends to zero. Their ledger must route into electron/positron ordered frames, relative product motion, recruited sea orientation, recoil/remnant response, and outgoing wakes. The corpus does not yet own polarization-resolved or differential near-threshold pair production, partial-wave/parity, or product-spin correlation rows, so the specific sharing remains an open geometry discriminator.

A reverse $e^-e^+\to\gamma\gamma$ control must be a separately prepared physical event with the complete product motion, frames, sea, wakes, remnants, and boundaries required by the inverse identity map $\Pi_{\widetilde{\mathsf e}}=\Pi_{\mathsf e}^{-1}$. Negating two velocities or running a movie backward is not the reverse experiment, and the delayed Master Equation is not presumed time-reversal invariant. Standard crossing and detailed balance remain effective recovery targets.

Plainly: formation and annihilation should interrogate the same morphology grammar, but the reverse test must restore every physical record exported by the forward event. Missing wakes or sea changes cannot be called fundamental irreversibility.

### Retained-Basin Transition Graph And Null Clues

Conservation closes the ledger of a candidate event, but it does not establish that a dynamically admissible route connects the parent and product basins. Let $B_\alpha$ and $B_\beta$ denote certified retained basins and define the candidate history set

$$
\mathcal P_{\alpha\to\beta}(\theta)
=
\left\{
\gamma:B_\alpha\leadsto B_\beta\;\middle|\;
\mathcal L_{E\mathbf p\mathbf J}(\gamma)=0,
\ \gamma\text{ closes exposure, sector, and identity provenance}
\right\}.
$$

Here $\mathcal L_{E\mathbf p\mathbf J}=0$ abbreviates the complete effective energy, momentum, and angular-momentum comparison ledger; it is not an imported architrino-level conservation law. A finite-window effective transition rate may then be compared through

$$
\Gamma_{\alpha\to\beta}(\theta;T_W)
=
\frac{1}{T_W}
\mu_{\theta,T_W}
\left(\mathcal P_{\alpha\to\beta}\right),
$$

where $\mu_{\theta,T_W}$ is an explicitly declared measure over prepared native histories or an empirical frequency over repeated preparations. It is not a primitive probability postulate. The native derivation must say whether this measure comes from basin volume, phase coverage, source preparation, sea-state variation, or another reproducible ensemble construction.

Plainly: a reaction can balance every account and still never occur because no actual trajectory reaches the products. Geometry must determine not only what is legal, but which routes exist and how broadly they are entered.

The same object separates four observer-level outcomes:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\text{allowed:}\quad
&\Gamma_{\alpha\to\beta}>0,\\
\text{suppressed:}\quad
&0<\Gamma_{\alpha\to\beta}\ll\Gamma_{\mathrm{ref}},\\
\text{forbidden in a declared regime:}\quad
&\Gamma_{\alpha\to\beta}=0,\\
\text{stable to a bound:}\quad
&\sum_{\beta\ne\alpha}\Gamma_{\alpha\to\beta}
<\Gamma_\alpha^{\max}.
\end{aligned}
$$

A zero or small rate does not uniquely reveal its cause. Candidate mechanisms include disconnected basin components, a symmetry cancellation, vanishing sector exposure, a narrow source-preparation set, a barrier whose crossing measure is below the declared bound, or failure of a required product basin to exist.
