\frac43\bigg[{}
&\sqrt6h_-\rho_-\dot\theta_-
\sin\!\left(\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{3}\right)
\\
&-\sqrt6b_-
\cos\!\left(\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{3}\right)
-\sqrt3\rho_-^2\dot\theta_-
\bigg],
\end{aligned}
$$

with $m_x=m_{x,+}+m_{x,-}$. Thus a magnetic-like axial current moment is not a function of one orbit frequency alone. It receives a circulation term from $\dot\theta_\sigma$ and a shear term from unequal fractional axial and radial breathing. The shear term vanishes when $h_\sigma$ and $\rho_\sigma$ expand or contract in strict proportion, but it can remain nonzero at an instant when $\dot\theta_\sigma=0$.

Plainly: regular circulation is one way to maintain the current axis, but it is not the only geometric motion that contributes to its magnitude. A tetrahedron that changes height and radius at different relative rates also carries an oriented internal current. Consequently, a future spin-facing frequency may be a full breathing-and-circulation return frequency rather than the count of simple turns around a fixed ring.

Its magnitude may breathe and even pass through zero; axis alignment alone does not establish a retained spin or magnetic moment.

The decisive experiment is not another prescribed circle. Release the eight-member F6c history under ordinary EOM evolution, reconstruct the six collective coordinates and their velocities from every accepted frame, and record:

1. the off-manifold residual relative to the polarity-resolved tetrahedral surface;
2. exact or bounded centroid and dipole leakage;
3. axial, radial, and phase turns for each polarity sector;
4. causal-root completeness, clearance, and field-speed margins;
5. return of all six coordinates and their rates on one section; and
6. current-axis survival and complete finite-window acceleration ledgers.

A periodic or recurrent candidate must additionally satisfy the same-record integral conditions

$$
\int_0^P H_\sigma\,dT=0,
\qquad
\int_0^P
\left(
R_\sigma+\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma^2
\right)dT=0,
$$

$$
\int_0^P\rho_\sigma Q_\sigma\,dT
=
\left[
\rho_\sigma^2\dot\theta_\sigma
\right]_0^P
=0,
$$

together with coordinate and rate return. These are necessary return conditions, not sufficient stability or retention proofs.

Plainly: F6c turns “frequency” into a concrete geometry program. A candidate frequency may be the return cadence of two coupled breathing tetrahedra, with circulation riding on top of axial and radial oscillation. The key clue is the polarity differential: tying the two sectors together destroys the natural Master Equation direction, while allowing them to breathe separately preserves the exact electric null and the body-fixed current axis.

#### F6c Uniform-Cadence Screen and First Ordinary Release

Before release, a bounded prescribed-path search tested whether unequal polarity-sector geometry could rescue a simpler uniform-cadence orbit. The search varied $\rho_+$ over $[0.18,0.48]$, both axial aspect ratios over $[0.5,2.5]$, $\rho_-/\rho_+$ over $[0.6,1.6]$, maximum member speed over $[0.15,0.75]$, and the complete negative-sector phase offset. Each candidate used one common positive acceleration coefficient across all members and all 12 cycle times. A deterministic 512-row Halton screen, followed by local coordinate refinement of the five best survivors, found no near-relative equilibrium. The best row retained normalized acceleration RMS residual $0.836251846$.

The frozen best row was reevaluated at 128 cycle times with tighter causal-root and geometry tolerances. Its normalized RMS residual was $0.836252421$, with the following orthogonal components:

| Residual direction | Normalized RMS |
| --- | ---: |
| axial | $0.325044315$ |
| radial | $0.726873467$ |
| phase | $0.255576344$ |

This is a bounded negative result for the declared common-uniform-cadence domain, not a no-go theorem for every F6c history. The executable record is [`f6c-relative-equilibrium-search.mjs`](../../../scripts/mapping-electromagnetism/f6c-relative-equilibrium-search.mjs).

Plainly: changing the two tetrahedra's sizes, heights, and relative phase does not make fixed circles follow the Master Equation in the searched domain. The remaining mismatch points in all three allowed collective directions, with radial response largest. Axial breathing and cadence change are therefore dynamically required in this seed family, not optional embellishments.

The accepted EOM harness now has a declared `f6c-balanced-tetrahedral-v1` seed family. It constructs the same eight prehistory paths from certified circular segments rotated onto the four tetrahedral axes, sets $c_f=1$, and releases them with the harness's previously declared coefficient. Release certification completed all 64 ordered receiver/transmitter certificate rows, including eight explicit self-channel bookkeeping rows, with no unresolved row. The release acceleration projections were:

| Sector | $H_\sigma$ | $R_\sigma$ | $Q_\sigma$ | Maximum within-sector spread |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| positive | $-0.1667274592$ | $-0.9980316808$ | $-0.3371501360$ | $6.11\times10^{-15}$ |
| negative | $-0.0261311866$ | $-0.7853096604$ | $-1.7056320450$ | $6.13\times10^{-15}$ |

The prescribed-path and EOM release projections agree after applying the declared charge/coefficient normalization. This is cross-implementation parity, not an independent validation of the Master Equation. The release host is [`attractor-ensemble-harness.cpp`](../../../scripts/eom/attractor-ensemble-harness.cpp), and the frame reduction is [`f6c-eom-coordinate-analysis.mjs`](../../../scripts/mapping-electromagnetism/f6c-eom-coordinate-analysis.mjs).

Plainly: the ordinary solver sees exactly the same six collective directions as the analytical diagnostic. More importantly, it can now evolve them rather than merely evaluate an imposed circle.

The first checkpointed release reached $T=0.374$. It accumulated 321 accepted steps and zero rejected steps before a deliberate operator stop at a clean checkpoint. The solver reduced its accepted step from $0.002$ to $0.0005$ as the history became harder. Across the accepted record:

1. normalized position leakage from the F6c surface stayed below $4.84\times10^{-16}$ and velocity leakage below $1.75\times10^{-15}$;
2. centroid norm stayed below $8.81\times10^{-17}$ and electric-dipole norm below $2.24\times10^{-16}$;
3. the current-axis off-$x$ fraction stayed below $5.17\times10^{-15}$;
4. accepted-chunk pair separation reached a minimum $0.3132131932$ near $T=0.146$ and then increased before decreasing again;
5. maximum member speed reached $0.6870583<c_f$; and
6. the axial current moment changed from $-0.4156922$ to $-0.1288454$ without losing its body axis.

The resolved collective events are more informative than the aggregate sizes. Negative-sector axial motion turned from contraction to expansion at $T\approx0.0236642$. Its phase rate then crossed zero at $T\approx0.1490220$, so that polarity sector reversed its internal circulation while the positive sector continued forward. The positive phase rate reached a minimum $0.8853460$ near $T=0.196$ and then increased to $1.0469475$ by $T=0.374$. Neither radius had turned by the checkpoint: $\rho_+=0.2505834$ and $\rho_-=0.2540948$ were still decreasing.

Plainly: ordinary evolution has now produced an axial breathing turn, a polarity-selective circulation reversal, and a cadence minimum while exact neutrality and the magnetic-like axis survive. That is a substantially richer geometry than an orbit. It is also not yet a retained braid: the radii have not returned, the six coordinates and rates have not closed, and the current moment has not completed a cycle.

A separate refinement used initial step $0.001$, minimum step $0.00025$, and half-size prehistory segments through $T=0.2$. It completed 200 accepted steps with no rejection. At the common endpoint, configuration-coordinate differences from the first run were at most $1.66\times10^{-8}$ and rate differences at most $2.45\times10^{-7}$. The axial-turn estimates differed by $1.47\times10^{-5}$, the phase-reversal estimates by $1.47\times10^{-6}$, and the two minimum-separation values by $5.12\times10^{-9}$.

A second launch tested whether the initial radial contraction could be removed by sector-resolved cadence. Solving the two release equations

$$
R_++\rho_+\dot\theta_+^2\approx0,
\qquad
R_-+\rho_-\dot\theta_-^2\approx0
$$

on the same $h_\pm=\rho_\pm=0.3$ geometry selected

$$
\dot\theta_+=2.345,
\qquad
\dot\theta_-=1.989.
$$

The corresponding member speeds were at most $0.7035<c_f$, and the certified release radial second derivatives were $-3.65\times10^{-4}$ and $-4.72\times10^{-4}$. The $T=0.2$ evolution completed 315 accepted steps with no rejection, but did not produce a radial turn. It ended with

$$
(\rho_+,\rho_-)=(0.2973306,0.2947164),
$$

while both axial scales expanded to about $0.3067$, the negative cadence fell to $0.7583$, and accepted-chunk pair separation fell to $0.2151674$.

Plainly: balancing radial acceleration at one release instant substantially reduced radius loss relative to the unit-cadence seed, but it did not create a cycle or even a radius turn. Delayed geometry immediately changes the radial response as phase, height, and cadence evolve. A retained braid must balance the complete history or return map, not one instantaneous acceleration row.

A nearby launch moved just beyond the simultaneous radial-balance row while remaining below the field-speed ceiling:

$$
\dot\theta_+=2.38,
\qquad
\dot\theta_-=2.00,
\qquad
\max_a|\mathbf V_a|=0.714<c_f.
$$

Both certified release radial second derivatives were positive. Ordinary evolution then resolved the first radial turns in this family: the negative sector reached a radius maximum at $T\approx0.001374$, while the positive sector reached one at $T\approx0.026625$. The excursions were only about $1.0\times10^{-9}$ and $4.5\times10^{-6}$ above the common release radius, respectively, before contraction resumed.

A higher-cadence launch,

$$
\dot\theta_+=3.0,
\qquad
\dot\theta_-=2.5,
\qquad
\max_a|\mathbf V_a|=0.9<c_f,
$$

produced a larger polarity-resolved breathing segment. Through the clean $T=0.152$ checkpoint, the negative radius reached $\rho_-=0.3012598721$ and turned from expansion to contraction at $T\approx0.135464$. The positive radius was still expanding at the checkpoint, with $\rho_+=0.3064302684$. The negative axial coordinate also turned near $T\approx0.056763$. Symmetry leakage remained below $5.13\times10^{-16}$ in position and $1.26\times10^{-15}$ in velocity; centroid, dipole, and current-axis leakage remained at roundoff scale. The minimum accepted-chunk pair separation fell to $0.1940721$, so the larger breathing excursion is accompanied by a tightening inter-member approach and does not establish retention.

The tightening corridor is not a conjugate-polarity approach. Direct frame reconstruction identifies the symmetry-equivalent negative-sector pair orbit $(0^-,1^-)$ and $(2^-,3^-)$ as the minimum-separation class in the unit-cadence, near-balanced, and outward launches. In the near-balanced record, for example, this same-polarity distance reached $0.2151674$ at $T=0.2$ even though the sector envelope radius remained $0.2947164$. The exact order-four symmetry makes the two listed pairs equivalent; neither is a unique local defect.

The negative-sector six-edge set splits into a two-edge orbit and a four-edge orbit. For the observed two-edge minimum, define $d_{-,2}$ as the common distance of $(0^-,1^-)$ and $(2^-,3^-)$. Direct substitution of the F6c coordinates gives the exact identity

$$
d_{-,2}^2
=
\frac49
\left(\sqrt3h_- - \sqrt6\rho_-\sin\theta_-\right)^2
+
\frac49
\left(
\sqrt3h_- - \sqrt6\rho_-\cos\left(\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{6}\right)
\right)^2.
$$

Consequently this edge orbit can reach coincidence only if

$$
h_-=\frac{\rho_-}{\sqrt2},
\qquad
\theta_-=\frac{\pi}{6}\pmod{2\pi}.
$$

This is a necessary and sufficient coincidence condition for that declared edge orbit, not a prediction that the evolved record reaches it. It provides an analytic clearance guard for future return-map searches. The other four negative-sector edges share

$$
d_{-,4}^2
=
\frac83h_-^2
+\frac{4\sqrt2}{3}h_-\rho_-\sin\left(\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{3}\right)
+\frac23\rho_-^2\sin\left(2\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{6}\right)
+\frac83\rho_-^2.
$$

The coordinate analyzer reevaluates both formulas at every emitted frame. On the near-balanced $T=0.2$ record, the maximum formula-to-member distance residual across both edge orbits was $4.45\times10^{-16}$.

Plainly: the dangerous same-polarity approach is not hidden in the full 24-dimensional member state. Within exact F6c it is an explicit function of negative-sector height, radius, and phase. A search can reject a candidate as soon as its reduced trajectory approaches the two simultaneous coincidence conditions, while still confirming the full root-resolved clearance ledger.

Relative polarity-sector phase is a physical F6c shape coordinate, not a choice of time origin. The EOM harness therefore exposes `f6c-negative-theta`, which changes $\theta_-(0)$ while holding $\theta_+(0)=0$, both cadences, every scale, the coefficient, and all solver controls fixed. A seven-row certified release screen at $(\dot\theta_+,\dot\theta_-)=(3.0,2.5)$ gave:

| $\theta_-(0)$ | Initial clearance | Closest-pair class | $\ddot\rho_+(0)$ | $\ddot\rho_-(0)$ |
| ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: |
| $-\pi/3$ | $0.251059$ | opposite polarity | $1.175832$ | $0.032569$ |
| $-\pi/6$ | $0.360963$ | opposite polarity | $0.034716$ | $0.556308$ |
| $0$ | $0.355051$ | same polarity | $0.803234$ | $0.468180$ |
| $\pi/6$ | $0.143488$ | same polarity | $1.488198$ | $-2.731117$ |
| $\pi/3$ | $0.355051$ | same polarity | $1.988540$ | $0.181116$ |
| $\pi/2$ | $0.366006$ | opposite polarity | $2.141275$ | $1.793673$ |
| $\pi$ | $0.077854$ | opposite polarity | $-21.143609$ | $-12.545210$ |

Plainly: one internal phase changes which members approach, whether each polarity envelope initially expands or contracts, and the response magnitude by more than an order of magnitude without changing the law or coefficient. Relative phase is therefore a load-bearing geometry variable and a plausible carrier of distinct response basins. The seven rows are a bounded phase screen, not evidence for seven states or any particle-family assignment.

The $\theta_-(0)=\pi/2$ row was evolved because it combined the largest release clearance with strong outward radial response in both sectors. It reached a clean $T=0.15$ checkpoint with 341 accepted steps and zero rejected steps. Neither radius nor height turned: the endpoint was

$$
(h_+,h_-)=(0.2920582,0.2844043),
\qquad
(\rho_+,\rho_-)=(0.3154963,0.3238952).
$$

The limiting corridor became the four-member opposite-polarity edge orbit $(0^+,1^-)$, $(0^-,1^+)$, $(2^+,3^-)$, and $(2^-,3^+)$, whose common distance fell from $0.3660060$ to $0.2559228$. Its exact reduced squared distance is

$$
\begin{aligned}
d_{\pm,4}^2={}&h_+^2-\frac23h_+h_-+h_-^2+\rho_+^2+\rho_-^2\\
&+\frac{4\sqrt2}{3}h_+\rho_-\sin\left(\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{3}\right)
-\frac{4\sqrt2}{3}h_-\rho_+\cos\left(\theta_++\frac{\pi}{6}\right)\\
&-\frac43\rho_+\rho_-\sin\theta_+\sin\theta_-
+\frac{2\sqrt3}{3}\rho_+\rho_-\sin(\theta_+-\theta_-).
\end{aligned}
$$

The framewise formula residual remained below $5.00\times10^{-16}$. Maximum member speed rose to $0.983222c_f$, leaving only $0.016778c_f$ of field-speed margin, so the record was not extended further.

Plainly: phase can redirect the dangerous corridor from same-polarity to opposite-polarity pairs, but the tested high-cadence row does not solve the return problem. It converts radial collapse into rapid radial expansion and approach to the field-speed ceiling. A retained branch must find a phase and cadence combination that turns both envelope and corridor motion before either coincidence or field-speed contact.

Plainly: cadence controls whether a sector initially contracts or expands and how large its first radial excursion becomes. More importantly, the two polarity sectors turn at different times. F6c therefore carries at least two coupled radial clocks: a common breathing coordinate and a polarity-differential breathing coordinate. Any retained version must phase-lock both clocks while also controlling a same-polarity edge-pinch coordinate that the sector radius does not determine by itself. Within F6c that pinch remains a derived function of the six coordinates, especially relative sector phase; it is not a seventh independent coordinate. One shared orbital frequency and two envelope radii are not enough.

A $4\times4$ certified release screen at $\theta_-(0)=\pi/2$ varied both cadences over $\{1.5,2.0,2.5,3.0\}$. The lower-speed compromise $(\dot\theta_+,\dot\theta_-)=(2.0,2.0)$ began at maximum member speed $0.6c_f$ with

$$
(\ddot\rho_+,\ddot\rho_-)=(0.6664443,0.2599755).
$$

Its $T=0.2$ evolution completed 283 accepted steps with no rejection. The positive radius reached $0.3036095$ and turned near $T=0.194186$; the negative radius reached $0.3065591$ and was still expanding. Meanwhile $\dot\theta_-$ rose from $2.0$ to $3.155066$, maximum member speed reached $0.973476c_f$, and the opposite-polarity edge corridor fell to $0.2180590$.

Plainly: lowering both initial cadences buys time but does not remove the negative-sector cadence pump. This row produces one genuine radius maximum, yet it reaches the field-speed neighborhood before the other radial clock turns.

A second nine-row release screen held both cadences at $2.0$ and sampled $\theta_-(0)=k\pi/16$ for $k=0,\ldots,8$. No sampled row simultaneously had small $|Q_-|$, outward radial response in both sectors, and generous clearance. The sampled sign change of $Q_-$ lies between $3\pi/16$ and $4\pi/16$, where the endpoint clearances were only $0.149473$ and $0.219615$ and the coordinate accelerations were already large. This is a bounded screen; it does not prove that no narrow admissible phase exists between samples.

The best sampled clearance/outward-response compromise was $\theta_-(0)=7\pi/16$, with release clearance $0.4037315$ and radial second derivatives $(0.7027147,0.1398979)$. Its $T=0.2$ evolution completed 244 accepted steps with no rejection, but again supplied no return: the endpoint had

$$
(\dot\rho_+,\dot\rho_-)=(0.0004236,0.1253897),
\qquad
\dot\theta_-=3.079583,
$$

maximum member speed $0.968961c_f$, and opposite-polarity corridor $0.2749050$.

Plainly: the favorable phase rows repeatedly approach a positive-sector radius maximum while the negative sector continues to expand and accelerate. The failure is structured, not random: phase chooses the corridor and cadence transfer, but a circular prehistory does not supply the missing breathing phase needed to close both sectors.

#### A Generic Breathing Section Has One Scalar Crossing

A fixed-radius circular prehistory imposes four simultaneous section conditions at release:

$$
\dot h_+=\dot h_-=\dot\rho_+=\dot\rho_-=0.
$$

A generic periodic six-coordinate history has only one freely selectable time origin. One time shift can generically place the history on one scalar section, such as $\dot\rho_+=0$, but not on four independent turning sections at once. Simultaneous satisfaction of all four conditions selects an exceptional synchronized subset unless a derived symmetry supplies that synchronization. This is a dimension-counting inference, not a nonexistence theorem.

The evolved $(2.0,2.0,\pi/2)$ history provides a measured example at its positive-radius section. Near $T=0.19425$ it had

$$
\dot\rho_+\approx-2.87\times10^{-5},
\qquad
(\dot h_+,\dot h_-,\dot\rho_-)
=
(-0.188826,-0.097687,0.055643),
$$

with sector cadences $(1.206147,3.101637)$. This is not a periodic return or a new seed certificate; it is a measured example of a legitimate one-coordinate section carrying nonzero motion in the other breathing coordinates.

Plainly: a return search should prescribe and certify a breathing path history, choose one Poincare section, and normally allow the other three axial and radial rates to remain nonzero. Four simultaneous turning points are exceptional unless an additional synchronization is proved.

Claim grade: measured EOM-solver diagnostics on three bounded release screens, seven distinct evolved releases, and one finite-time refinement, plus an inferred section-dimension argument; not an independent oracle, retained braid, stability result, particle identity, or global basin. Falsifier: failure to reproduce the coordinate records from the declared seeds, nonconvergent turn times or coordinates under further refinement, loss of complete roots, a nonvanishing symmetry leakage under refinement, or a certified breathing return family whose trajectories generically cross all four zero-rate sections simultaneously.

#### Certified Harmonic Breathing Histories

The next instrument replaces the four-zero-rate circular section by the one-frequency coordinate history

$$
\begin{aligned}
h_\sigma(T)&=0.3+A_{h,\sigma}\sin(\Omega T),\\
\rho_\sigma(T)&=0.3+A_{\rho,\sigma}\sin(\Omega T),\\
\theta_+(T)&=\omega_+T,\\
\theta_-(T)&=\theta_{-,0}+\omega_-T.
\end{aligned}
$$

The first bounded screens set $\Omega=\omega_+=\omega_-$ so each prescribed coordinate history has one exact recurrence period. Piecewise cubic Hermite segments carry analytical endpoint positions and velocities, conservative position/velocity enclosure errors, and a whole-history speed bound. Every admitted release must keep $h_\sigma>0$, $\rho_\sigma>0$, maximum prescribed member speed below $c_f=1$, and all 64 ordered causal-root bookkeeping rows certified.

Plainly: this is the smallest periodic breathing history that reaches the same $h_\sigma=\rho_\sigma=0.3$ release geometry with nonzero axial and radial rates. It tests whether the delayed past, not only the instantaneous positions, changes the Master Equation response.

The acceleration analyzer now separates physical projections from coordinate second derivatives. With $H_\sigma$, $R_\sigma$, and $Q_\sigma$ denoting the axial, radial, and signed tangential projections on the F6c moving frame,

$$
\ddot h_\sigma=H_\sigma,
\qquad
\ddot\rho_\sigma=R_\sigma+\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma^2,
\qquad
\ddot\theta_\sigma
=
\frac{Q_\sigma-2\dot\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma}{\rho_\sigma}.
$$

The $\rho\dot\theta^2$ and $2\dot\rho\dot\theta$ terms are moving-coordinate geometry, not additional Master Equation interactions. Consequently, $R_\sigma<0$ alone does not imply radial contraction; the stricter local condition is $R_\sigma<-\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma^2$.

Plainly: an inward acceleration vector can still accompany an increasing orbit radius because part of that acceleration supplies the curvature of the circulation. The diagnostic must report $\ddot\rho$, not label the radial projection $R$ as a radius acceleration.

Two disjoint low-discrepancy screens tested 144 declared histories. Of 111 speed-admissible, root-certified rows, 48 had $\ddot\rho_+<0$ and $\ddot\rho_-<0$ at release; ten of those also began with $\dot\rho_+>0$ and $\dot\rho_->0$. These counts describe only the bounded screen and its declared ranges. They do not estimate a basin volume.

One fixed row from the first screen predicted two radial maxima. Its full EOM continuation certified 478 accepted steps and no rejected steps through the durable $T=0.195$ checkpoint. It resolved

$$
T_{\rho_+,\max}\approx0.0476328,
\qquad
T_{\rho_-,\max}\approx0.0626490.
$$

No axial turn occurred in that window. Maximum member speed was $0.773405c_f$, minimum accepted-chunk pair distance was $0.265661$, and the maximum normalized position and velocity leakage from F6c was respectively $4.91\times10^{-16}$ and $9.31\times10^{-16}$.

A second fixed row was the only sampled release that combined two outward radial rates, two negative radial second derivatives, and axial acceleration opposite to axial velocity in both sectors. Through its durable $T=0.09$ checkpoint, it certified 252 accepted steps with no rejected step and resolved

$$
T_{h_-,\max}\approx0.0264644,
\qquad
T_{h_+,\max}\approx0.0792469.
$$

Neither radius turned in that window. Maximum member speed was $0.743785c_f$, minimum accepted-chunk pair distance was $0.177735$, and maximum normalized position and velocity leakage was respectively $4.05\times10^{-16}$ and $1.03\times10^{-15}$. In both histories the polarity-weighted current moment remained on the exact body axis to numerical precision.

Plainly: the same eight-member geometry supports controlled radial turning in one history and controlled axial turning in another. The local acceleration screen found both directions efficiently, but it did not predict the complete finite-time outcome: delayed response can change before a rate reaches zero. The next problem is to phase-lock already demonstrated motions, not to add an unmotivated extra coordinate.

The correct next search object is therefore a Poincare return map on the exact six-coordinate history surface. Choose one scalar section, evolve one complete root-valid history to its next crossing, and minimize the remaining coordinate and rate mismatch while enforcing the analytical pair-corridor formulas and the $c_f=1$ speed margin. A release-only acceleration screen is an economical prefilter for that map, not a retention or recurrence certificate.

Claim grade: measured EOM-solver diagnostics from two bounded release screens and two finite ordinary evolutions, plus derived moving-coordinate identities; not an independent oracle, retained braid, stable cycle, particle identity, or basin measure. Falsifier: failure to reproduce the declared release and turn records, turn times that fail step/history refinement, a symmetry residual that grows under refinement, an unreported unresolved causal root, or a complete return-map result showing that the apparent axial/radial channels do not coexist on the same invariant surface.

#### Whole-Cycle Self-Consistency Residual

A release that turns after evolution need not make its prescribed prehistory a self-sustaining cycle. To test the stronger statement, sample one immutable harmonic history at phases $\phi_j=2\pi j/N$ and compare its analytical coordinate acceleration with the Master Equation acceleration generated by that same complete history:

$$
\mathbf r_\sigma(\phi_j)
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\ddot h_\sigma^{\mathrm{EOM}}\\
\ddot\rho_\sigma^{\mathrm{EOM}}\\
\ddot\theta_\sigma^{\mathrm{EOM}}
\end{pmatrix}_{\phi_j}
-
\begin{pmatrix}
-A_{h,\sigma}\Omega^2\sin\phi_j\\
-A_{\rho,\sigma}\Omega^2\sin\phi_j\\
0
\end{pmatrix}.
$$

Every phase uses the same amplitudes, cadence, relative phase, coupling, and root policy. The scalar ranking is the RMS of all six component residuals over all certified phases; maximum component residual, minimum pair distance, and maximum member speed remain separate and cannot be traded into that RMS.

Plainly: this asks whether the proposed wave generates the acceleration needed to keep tracing itself. It is stronger than finding an attractive acceleration at one point and cheaper than evolving every poor candidate for a full period.

The two histories that produced finite-time turns are not self-sustaining sine waves. At 12 phases, the double-radial-turn history had RMS residual $9.895$ and minimum phase clearance $0.1124$; the double-axial-turn history had RMS residual $16.208$ and clearance $0.1111$. Their observed turns remain valid, but their originally prescribed waveforms fail the full-cycle test.

An initial 24-row, eight-phase search appeared to reduce the RMS to $3.773$. Freezing that row and doubling phase resolution raised the RMS to $7.022$ and exposed a $0.1202$ corridor. The apparent improvement was phase-grid aliasing, so no eight-phase row was promoted.

Two disjoint 12-phase global screens followed by two local refinements found a cluster with cadence approximately $0.9$--$1.0$, relative sector phase approximately $1.1$--$1.3$, opposite-signed axial amplitudes near $A_{h,+}\approx0.11$ and $A_{h,-}\approx-0.13$, and smaller approximately opposite radial amplitudes. Its best 12-phase RMS was $2.797$; a frozen 24-phase confirmation gave $2.898$, maximum residual $10.427$, minimum clearance $0.2154$, and maximum member speed $0.3194c_f$.

Plainly: the bounded search found a reproducible direction, but not a nearly self-consistent cycle. The remaining mismatch is far larger than the numerical enclosures and cannot be described as convergence to a retained orbit.

Because cadence acceleration dominated the residual, the next minimal ansatz allowed one periodic phase modulation per sector,

$$
\theta_\sigma(T)
=
\omega T+\theta_{\sigma,0}+B_\sigma\cos(\Omega T),
$$

with the whole-history speed bound using $|\dot\theta_\sigma|\le |\omega|+|B_\sigma|\Omega$. A local search favored small, nearly common negative modulation, $B_+\approx-0.086$ and $B_-\approx-0.089$. Its best 12-phase RMS was $2.682$, but 24-phase confirmation gave $2.816$, maximum residual $10.518$, clearance $0.2249$, and maximum member speed $0.3160c_f$.

The modest confirmed improvement supports cadence breathing as a real search coordinate, consistent with the large opposite cadence changes in ordinary evolution. It does not rescue the one-frequency sine/cosine waveform. The parameter cluster is a seed for a nonlinear, evolved return map in which the waveform is output, not an imposed harmonic template.

Claim grade: measured EOM-solver bounded prescribed-history searches and frozen higher-phase confirmations; not an independent oracle, global optimum, evolved return, retention, stability, or particle identity. Falsifier: a rerun that does not reproduce the phase rows, a refined row whose residual converges to the enclosure scale, a denser confirmation that reveals unresolved roots, or a nonlinear evolved return showing that the reported cluster is unrelated to the actual recurrent branch.

#### First Nonlinear Section Return And Cadence Root Boundary

The best frozen phase-modulated harmonic row was released into ordinary EOM evolution, so its subsequent waveform was generated by the delayed dynamics rather than constrained to the prescribed sine/cosine template. The positive axial coordinate first turned near $T=0.140865$ and then crossed its initial level $h_+=0.3$ in the decreasing direction at

$$
T_{h_+=0.3}\approx0.272382754.
$$

This was a section return, not a state return. At the crossing, the RMS of the other eleven coordinate-and-rate differences was $0.825221$, and the maximum difference was the negative-sector cadence error $\Delta\dot\theta_-=2.591159$. The positive-sector cadence had changed by $-0.593993$, while the negative relative phase had advanced by $0.577353$.

Plainly: one geometric coordinate came back to its starting value, but the assembly did not come back to its starting motion. The delayed interaction transferred cadence strongly between the two polarity sectors during the excursion.

Continuation remained exactly on the F6c surface to normalized position and velocity residuals $4.72\times10^{-16}$ and $8.30\times10^{-16}$. The centroid and dipole stayed null to numerical precision, the body-fixed current axis stayed aligned to $1.36\times10^{-15}$, and minimum pair distance remained $0.296293$. Nevertheless, the negative-sector cadence rose to $\dot\theta_-=3.616172$ and its member speed reached $0.999765c_f$ at the last accepted state, $T=0.28625$.

The attempted next step was rejected with `root_completeness_not_certified`: the four symmetry-equivalent negative-sector self-history rows all reported `numeric_transmitter_factor_sign_uncertified` on a required caustic route. This is not evidence of collision, off-surface breakup, or a certified caustic. At measured grade, it is a sector-local approach to the causal-root margin coincident with the field-speed boundary; the uncertified transmitter-factor sign is consistent with a nearby fold but does not classify one.

Plainly: the candidate remained a coherent neutral shape, but one internal polarity sector accelerated almost to the wake speed. Its own delayed-history intersection then became too difficult to enclose completely. A viable recurrent braid must turn, reverse, or redistribute that cadence before this boundary closes.

This failure sharpens the return-map objective. A candidate cannot be ranked only by position recurrence or harmonic acceleration residual. Its section residual must include both sector cadences, and its admissibility guards must include the minimum causal-root transmitter-factor margin in addition to pair clearance and member speed. Opposite-signed axial breathing and small common cadence modulation remain useful seed coordinates, but they do not by themselves regulate the nonlinear polarity-differential cadence exchange.

Claim grade: measured EOM-solver diagnostic on one fixed, root-certified release and its ordinary continuation; not an independent oracle, retained braid, periodic orbit, stability result, universal speed-ceiling theorem, or particle identity. Falsifier: failure to reproduce the crossing and halt from the declared record, convergence of the section residual toward zero under step/history refinement, a refined complete-root continuation through the reported event with substantial speed margin, or identification of an unrelated numerical defect that caused the four self-history failures.

#### Nontrivial Return Markers

A local cycle-phase screen showed that release phase can regulate the cadence failure. Three prescribed-history phases with negative release $\ddot\theta_-$ produced first-section residual RMS values $0.3401$, $0.4592$, and $0.3216$, all below the phase-zero value $0.8252$. The best row's $h_-$-section crossing at $T\approx0.173457$ reproduced under half-step refinement to $9.8\times10^{-7}$ in crossing time and $1.4\times10^{-6}$ in residual RMS. On one fixed $h_+$ section, phases $4.80$, $4.85$, $4.90$, and $5.05$ gave RMS values $0.3221$, $0.3229$, $0.3271$, and $0.3625$.

Plainly: choosing a better point on the same prescribed history prevents the immediate cadence runaway, but phase tuning reaches a floor near $0.32$. Most of the remaining error is transfer into opposite radial rates, so release phase is a useful regulator rather than the missing recurrence mechanism.

Reducing both prescribed radial amplitudes at phase $4.80$ appeared to improve the first-crossing residual dramatically: half amplitude gave $0.1074$, and zero radial amplitude gave $0.05741$. The latter crossing, however, occurred after only $T=0.04271$. Its maximum $h_+$ excursion from the section was only $9.41\times10^{-5}$, and its lifted phase advances were only $0.03648$ and $0.03208$ radians.

This exposes a degeneracy in an unconstrained first-section objective. Near a tangent release, a coordinate can make an arbitrarily short local excursion and recross its initial level before the rest of the geometry changes much. The residual then shrinks with flight time even though no braid period has been completed.

Plainly: the small number came from a tiny out-and-back arc, not from the assembly completing a meaningful cycle. A scalar section crossing cannot by itself define the frequency of a braid.

The analyzer therefore records flight time, maximum section excursion, and both lifted phase advances for every initial-level crossing. A nontrivial return search must arm its Poincare map with at least one topology- or identity-aware cycle marker, such as a declared lifted winding, an ordered frame or member-permutation return, a required sequence of axial/radial turns, or a scale-aware excursion floor. The correct marker remains to be selected from the exact F6c identity and symmetry action; an arbitrary minimum time is only a numerical guard.

Continuation of the zero-radial-history row beyond the short recrossing found no larger return through the durable $T=0.36$ checkpoint. The negative circulation reversed near $T=0.21542$, while $\rho_+$ expanded and $\rho_-$ contracted. The lifted phase advances at $T=0.36$ were only $0.33263$ and $0.01001$ radians. Maximum member speed reached $0.91983c_f$, and the limiting opposite-polarity edge orbit narrowed to $0.21782$. Root work rose to 1,396 MPFR pairs in the final accepted chunk, so the run was stopped recoverably without claiming a physical halt.

Plainly: after its tiny local recrossing, this row separates into opposite radial motion and approaches an opposite-polarity edge pinch. It does not hide a larger cycle immediately behind the low residual.

Claim grade: measured EOM-solver diagnostics on a bounded release-phase and radial-amplitude neighborhood, with one half-step crossing refinement; not an independent oracle, complete local optimization, periodic orbit, stability result, retained braid, or particle identity. Falsifier: failure to reproduce the declared crossings, nonconvergence of the refined crossing, discovery of a nontrivial identity/winding return before the reported obstruction, or a properly armed return map for which the near-tangent rows remain genuine low-residual full cycles.

#### Exact F6c Return Actions

The return marker can be derived from the declared F6c member map rather than chosen numerically. Enumerating the 24 signed-coordinate orthogonal maps that preserve the tetrahedral module axes gives eight maps that preserve the F6c common-phase chart. Four are proper rotations. On the two polarity-sector phases, those four rotations reduce to only two distinct actions:

$$
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{direct}}:
(\theta_+,\theta_-)
\longmapsto
(\theta_+,\theta_-),
$$

and

$$
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{reflected}}:
(\theta_+,\theta_-)
\longmapsto
\left(-\theta_+-\frac{\pi}{3},
-\theta_-+\frac{\pi}{3}\right).
$$

The direct ordered return therefore requires, for integers $k_+$ and $k_-$,

$$
\theta_\sigma(T)=\theta_\sigma(0)+2\pi k_\sigma,
\qquad
\dot\theta_\sigma(T)=\dot\theta_\sigma(0),
$$

together with return of both $h_\sigma$, both $\rho_\sigma$, and all four of their rates. The reflected geometric return instead requires

$$
\begin{aligned}
\theta_+(T)&=-\theta_+(0)-\frac{\pi}{3}+2\pi k_+,\\
\theta_-(T)&=-\theta_-(0)+\frac{\pi}{3}+2\pi k_-,\\
\dot\theta_\sigma(T)&=-\dot\theta_\sigma(0),
\end{aligned}
$$

with the same axial, radial, and rate returns. Each reflected action combines a proper spatial rotation with a nontrivial permutation of same-polarity modules. It is therefore a valid orientation-quotiented shape test, but it is not automatically an ordered path-history return. Treating the module permutation as physical identity requires a separate exchange-history argument.

Plainly: F6c supplies two exact ways for the visible geometry to look the same again. One brings every labeled motion back directly. The other brings the shape back only after a rotation, a module exchange, and reversal of both sector circulations. Those are not the same claim about a braid.

The zero-radial release through $T=0.36$ populated only the direct zero-winding cell. Its smallest direct residual was the first stored frame at $T=0.002$, another near-release degeneracy. Its closest reflected residual occurred at $T=0.297$ with RMS $0.844798$ and maximum component $1.752080$. At the final checkpoint the direct and reflected RMS values were respectively $0.754922$ and $0.891503$. Thus the record reaches neither a nonzero direct winding nor even an approximate reflected return.

Plainly: the exact symmetry calculation rejects the attractive short-return number and supplies a discrete target for subsequent shooting. A candidate period must land in a declared winding cell and state whether module exchange counts; minimizing elapsed time or one wrapped angle is no longer admissible.

Claim grade: derived finite-group geometry for the exact F6c chart, plus measured EOM-solver residuals on one declared trajectory. The enumeration script is an executable check of the algebra, not an independent physical oracle. Falsifier: direct substitution showing an omitted proper rotation or a different phase action, failure of a listed action to reproduce all eight member positions and velocities, or a trajectory record whose independently reconstructed ordered state contradicts the reported residual.

#### Existing-Record Return Census And Reflected Boundary

The exact action test was applied to all 2,451 stored F6c manifests. Only 15 were evolved records with positive accepted time and usable frame streams. None entered a nonzero direct winding cell. The closest reflected-action row was the cycle-phase-$5.5$ release. Its minimum reflected residual occurred at the record endpoint, so that same immutable history was continued rather than starting a newly tuned row.

The reflected RMS changed from $0.545956$ at $T=0.20$ to $0.536961$ at $T=0.24$, $0.535204$ at $T=0.25$, and $0.535034$ at the last accepted state, $T=0.251$. This small scalar improvement did not represent convergence of the complete state. The last reflected residual still contained

$$
\Delta\dot\theta_+=1.238199,
\qquad
\Delta\dot\rho_-=-0.964811,
\qquad
\Delta\dot\theta_-=0.694174.
$$

Meanwhile the negative radius fell to $0.210056$, its radial rate reached $-0.994208$, and maximum member speed reached $0.998557c_f$. The attempted next step failed complete-root certification on the four symmetry-equivalent negative self-history rows with `numeric_transmitter_factor_sign_uncertified`. Pair clearance in the last accepted chunk was $0.312716$, so the certified obstruction was the sector-local causal boundary rather than member collision.

Plainly: one wrapped view of the shape was becoming modestly more similar to a rotated start, but its motion was not. The negative tetrahedral sector was collapsing radially at nearly the wake speed. A useful reflected return needs the radial and cadence reversals to be coordinated; allowing one sector to reach the causal boundary can make a shape-only residual look better without making a braid cycle.

This result adds a sharper geometry requirement. A recurrent F6c branch must include a native exchange among axial breathing, radial breathing, and circulation that turns the sector-local speed before the relevant self-history transmitter factor becomes uncertifiable. The action residual must therefore remain component-resolved and be constrained by a causal-margin guard; its RMS cannot be optimized alone.

Claim grade: measured census of existing EOM-solver records and continuation of one fixed, root-certified history; not an independent oracle, exhaustive parameter search, periodic orbit, retained braid, or particle identity. Falsifier: an eligible stored record omitted by the census, failure to reproduce the ranked residuals, refined continuation that passes the reported event with complete roots and substantial speed margin, or a nearby fixed-law row that reaches the exact action with all component residuals converging before its guards close.

#### F6c Sector Speed Budget

The F6c moving frame is orthonormal. Every member of polarity sector $\sigma$ therefore has the same exact squared speed,

$$
v_\sigma^2
=
\dot h_\sigma^2
+
\dot\rho_\sigma^2
+
\left(\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma\right)^2.
$$

In normalized units an admissible history must keep this sum below one. The three nonnegative terms are an exact kinematic budget for axial breathing, radial breathing, and circulation. They are not separately bounded reservoirs: growth in any one channel reduces the speed margin available to the other two.

The two root-boundary histories occupy almost opposite corners of this budget. At $T=0.28625$ in the phase-zero row, the negative sector had

$$
(f_h,f_\rho,f_\theta)
\approx
(0.06340,0.0000065,0.93659),
$$

where each $f$ is that channel's fraction of $v_-^2$. At $T=0.251$ in the phase-$5.5$ row, the fractions were

$$
(f_h,f_\rho,f_\theta)
\approx
(0.00771,0.99131,0.00098).
$$

The coordinate formula reproduced all measured member speeds in both records within $10^{-15}$. Thus one history reached the causal margin primarily by circulation and the other primarily by radial collapse.

Plainly: the wake-speed boundary does not merely prohibit an architrino from orbiting too quickly. It also prohibits a braid from breathing inward or outward too quickly. A retained clock must continually exchange motion among its shape channels while keeping their combined speed inside the unit sphere.

This suggests a useful representation for future searches: track each sector through the nonnegative speed-fraction triangle together with the remaining margin $1-v_\sigma^2$. A recurrent braid must form a closed, guarded history in full geometry; its projection in this triangle can expose whether it has a genuine mode-exchange cycle or simply runs toward one boundary vertex. The triangle is a diagnostic projection, not a binding potential or conservation law.

Claim grade: exactly derived kinematics on the F6c surface and measured channel fractions on two EOM-solver records. Falsifier: direct reconstruction of an F6c member velocity that violates the orthogonal sum, a member-speed spread in an exact F6c frame above numerical enclosure, or an independent coordinate extraction that contradicts the reported fractions.

#### Shape-Dependent Current Capacity

The speed budget and exact current formula can be combined without importing an observer-level magnetic law. Define the orthonormal rate vector

$$
\mathbf q_\sigma
=
\left(\dot h_\sigma,
\dot\rho_\sigma,
\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma\right),
\qquad
\|\mathbf q_\sigma\|=v_\sigma.
$$

For $\alpha=\theta_++\pi/6$ and $\beta=\theta_-+\pi/3$, define

$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathbf a_+
={}&\left(
-\sqrt6\rho_+\sin\alpha,
\sqrt6h_+\sin\alpha,
\sqrt6h_+\cos\alpha+\sqrt3\rho_+
\right),\\
\mathbf a_-
={}&\left(
\sqrt6\rho_-\cos\beta,
-\sqrt6h_-\cos\beta,
\sqrt6h_-\sin\beta-\sqrt3\rho_-
\right).
\end{aligned}
$$

Then the two exact sector current moments are

$$
m_{x,+}=-\frac43\mathbf a_+\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf q_+,
\qquad
m_{x,-}=\frac43\mathbf a_-\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf q_-.
$$

The unit-speed constraint immediately gives the shape-dependent bounds

$$
|m_{x,\sigma}|
\le
\frac43\|\mathbf a_\sigma\|v_\sigma
<
\frac43\|\mathbf a_\sigma\|.
$$

The final quantity is the instantaneous unit-speed current capacity of that sector geometry. Equality at a fixed speed requires the rate vector to align or anti-align with $\mathbf a_\sigma$. The two-dimensional plane perpendicular to $\mathbf a_\sigma$ carries internal F6c motion but contributes zero to that sector's axial current moment at the same instant.

Plainly: a shape does not acquire a large magnetic-like moment merely because its architrinos move quickly. For each shape there is one most efficient blend of axial breathing, radial breathing, and circulation. Two other blends can consume speed while remaining invisible to this current projection.

The two causal-boundary records illustrate the distinction. In the cadence-dominated negative sector, the unit-speed capacity was $1.208121$, the measured current magnitude was $0.171359$, and the alignment fraction at the actual speed was $0.141873$. In the radial-collapse negative sector, the capacity was $0.841231$, the measured current magnitude was only $0.009732$, and the alignment fraction was $0.011586$. The second row used almost the entire speed budget in a nearly current-null direction.

This supplies a magnetic-geometry discriminator that speed and frequency do not. A candidate intended to support a persistent current moment should be ranked by its complete-cycle current output per causal speed margin, while a neutral mode may deliberately lie near a current-null plane. Neither criterion establishes binding or identifies a Standard Model particle, but both reject the inference that rapid circulation or breathing is sufficient by itself.

Claim grade: exact F6c kinematic derivation, independently checked by summing all eight member cross products, plus measured values on two EOM-solver records. Falsifier: a direct member sum that disagrees with either dot-product formula, violation of the Cauchy--Schwarz bound on a root-valid F6c frame, or an independently reconstructed record that contradicts the stated capacities or alignment fractions.

#### Shape Period Versus Current Period

The two reflected proper-rotation actions are order two. Each is a half-turn about a body axis transverse to the F6c current axis, and therefore

$$
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{reflected}}\hat{\mathbf x}
=
-\hat{\mathbf x},
\qquad
\mathcal R_{\mathrm{reflected}}^2=\mathcal I.
$$

Because the exact F6c current moment lies on $\hat{\mathbf x}$, an exact relative return under either reflected action obeys

$$
\mathbf m_{\mathrm{cur}}(T+\tau)
=
-\mathbf m_{\mathrm{cur}}(T),
\qquad
\mathbf m_{\mathrm{cur}}(T+2\tau)
=
\mathbf m_{\mathrm{cur}}(T).
$$

The same action reverses both cadences and exchanges same-polarity modules. Thus its reduced shape period is $\tau$, while its fixed-frame current period is $2\tau$. If the half-cycle symmetry is exact throughout the history, its fixed-frame current average over the doubled period is zero.

Plainly: a braid can repeat its shape before it repeats its magnetic posture. After one reflected half-cycle the tetrahedron is the same up to rotation and module exchange, but its current points the other way. Two such steps are needed to restore the current.

The direct action has order one in the F6c phase chart and preserves the current axis. It is therefore the relevant action for a fixed-frame persistent current posture. A reflected relative periodic orbit could still describe an alternating internal mode, but it cannot by itself supply a nonzero persistent fixed-frame current unless another geometric feature breaks the half-cycle cancellation or the recovered observable follows a separately justified body frame.

This distinction matters whenever a recovered frequency is inferred from a geometric recurrence. A shape-sensitive observable can respond at twice the frequency of a current-sensitive observable on an exact reflected orbit. The factor of two is a derived symmetry consequence in F6c, not a general claim about spin, a quantum postulate, or evidence for a particular particle.

Claim grade: derived proper-rotation and current-vector geometry on the exact F6c chart. Falsifier: a listed reflected action that does not square to the identity, does not send $\hat{\mathbf x}$ to $-\hat{\mathbf x}$, or fails to reverse the directly summed current moment of a transformed F6c state.

#### Observable-Specific Return Order

The F6c factor of two is an instance of a general relative-return rule. Let a complete delayed geometry $Z(T)$ obey

$$
Z(T+\tau)=gZ(T)
$$

for one declared symmetry action $g$. If an observable projection $\mathcal O$ transforms by a representation $D_{\mathcal O}$,

$$
\mathcal O(gZ)=D_{\mathcal O}(g)\mathcal O(Z),
$$

then its first symmetry-forced return occurs after $k_{\mathcal O}\tau$, where $k_{\mathcal O}$ is the least positive integer for which $D_{\mathcal O}(g)^{k_{\mathcal O}}$ acts as the identity on that observable. A special observable state can return sooner, so this is a symmetry order, not an assertion that every Fourier component has that fundamental period.

For the reflected F6c action the executable return ledger is

| Projection | Action after $\tau$ | Return order |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Orientation-quotiented scalar shape | unchanged | $1$ |
| Sector cadence vector | sign reversed | $2$ |
| Fixed-frame axial current | sign reversed | $2$ |

Plainly: several measured frequencies can come from one geometric clock. The assembly need not contain a separate orbit for each frequency; different measurements can require different numbers of the same relative-return step before they repeat.

This supplies a disciplined inference rule for Standard Model geometry. Integer frequency ratios should first be tested against observable return orders, member permutations, and harmonics of one complete delayed cycle before adding independent internal clocks. Conversely, incommensurate or history-incompatible frequencies remain evidence that one return coordinate is insufficient.

F6c does not yet recover spinor return behavior. Its reflected action is an ordinary order-two spatial rotation on vectors. A $4\pi$ spinor recovery target would require a separately derived nontrivial lift of the ordered history action whose relevant representation has the required higher return order. The present factor of two is therefore a clue about return bookkeeping, not evidence that F6c is a fermion or that classical vector reversal is a spinor sign.

Claim grade: derived finite-group return bookkeeping. Falsifier: an observable whose directly transformed value disagrees with its recorded action, an incorrect group order, or an evolved relative periodic record whose independently reconstructed observable sequence contradicts the predicted return order.

#### Current Transport, Guard Flow, And A Causal-History Boundary

A release-frame current magnitude is not enough to identify a useful internal current geometry. Write the sector rate vector and current coefficient as

$$
\mathbf q_\sigma
=
(\dot h_\sigma,\dot\rho_\sigma,
\rho_\sigma\dot\theta_\sigma),
\qquad
I_\sigma
=
s_\sigma\frac43\mathbf a_\sigma(Z)\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf q_\sigma,
$$

where $s_+=-1$, $s_-=+1$, and $\mathbf a_\sigma$ is the exact shape-dependent current coefficient derived above. Its instantaneous flow is

$$
\dot I_\sigma
=
s_\sigma\frac43
\left(
\dot{\mathbf a}_\sigma\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf q_\sigma
+
\mathbf a_\sigma\mathbin{\cdot}\dot{\mathbf q}_\sigma
\right).
$$

The two terms separate rotation of the shape-dependent current direction from Master Equation acceleration of the sector rate vector. A current-supporting candidate must therefore control at least five distinct release quantities: current magnitude and alignment, pair clearance, limiting-corridor distance rate, field-speed margin and speed derivative, and $\|\dot{\mathbf q}_\sigma\|$. A decreasing speed does not imply a gentle trajectory because $\dot{\mathbf q}_\sigma$ can be almost perpendicular to $\mathbf q_\sigma$.

Plainly: a braid can carry a large current at one instant while already being aimed to lose it, collide, or sharply redirect its internal motion. Current, clearance, and motion smoothness are separate requirements.

There is also an exact assembly-level separation between current-carrying and current-neutral motion. Define

$$
\mathbf Q=(\mathbf q_+,\mathbf q_-),
\qquad
\mathbf C(Z)=
\left(-\frac43\mathbf a_+,\frac43\mathbf a_-\right),
\qquad
I_{\mathrm{tot}}=\mathbf C\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf Q.
$$

At any fixed nonnull shape and target current $I_0$, every admissible rate vector has the unique orthogonal decomposition

$$
\mathbf Q
=
\underbrace{\frac{I_0}{\|\mathbf C\|^2}\mathbf C}_{
\text{minimum-norm current carrier}}
+
\underbrace{\mathbf Q_\perp}_{\text{current-neutral motion}},
\qquad
\mathbf C\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf Q_\perp=0.
$$

Thus a specified instantaneous current leaves a five-dimensional affine hyperplane of collective rate choices. The first term is the unique minimum of the Euclidean six-coordinate rate norm; it does not by itself minimize the largest member speed or satisfy a causal-history guard. The orthogonal term can carry breathing, phase redistribution, and return-control motion without changing the instantaneous current at that shape.

Plainly: one rate combination carries the requested current most economically, but five independent combinations are invisible to that current readout. Those hidden motions are where a braid can protect separation and arrange a return while keeping the same instantaneous current.

The same construction can include one pair corridor. At fixed shape, the distance rate of pair $p$ is another exact linear row,

$$
\dot d_p=\mathbf D_p(Z)\mathbin{\cdot}\mathbf Q.
$$

If $\mathbf C$ and $\mathbf D_p$ are independent, target current $I_0$ and target corridor rate $\delta_0$ have the unique minimum-norm carrier

$$
\mathbf Q_*
=
A^{\mathsf T}(AA^{\mathsf T})^{-1}
\begin{pmatrix}I_0\\ \delta_0\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
A=
\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf C^{\mathsf T}\\
\mathbf D_p^{\mathsf T}\end{pmatrix}.
$$

Every other solution is $\mathbf Q_*+\mathbf Q_{\perp 2}$ with $A\mathbf Q_{\perp 2}=0$. Current plus one corridor condition therefore leave a four-dimensional joint-neutral rate space.

For the selected strict-history release shape, the current-only minimum-norm carrier for $I_0=-0.728215$ had collective norm $0.333269$ but would have closed the limiting pair at rate $-0.200974$. The minimum carrier satisfying the same current and the nonclosing boundary $\dot d_p=0$ had norm $0.368445$ and maximum sector member speed $0.337982$. Matching the observed opening rate $+0.195976$ required minimum collective norm $0.455370$. The actual release norm was $0.532463$, leaving a four-dimensional joint-neutral residual of norm $0.275963$.

Plainly: maximizing current efficiency points this shape toward collision. Some current-neutral motion is mathematically necessary just to stop the pair from closing, and more is needed to open it. The remaining four directions are the rate freedom available for return and additional guards.

This is a fixed-shape kinematic construction. A projected rate vector must still be embedded in a matching delayed prehistory, pass complete-root certification, and evolve under the unchanged Master Equation. It is a seed generator and lower bound, not evidence that the projected motion is retained.

The expanded release census tested whether this tradeoff recurs. Of 1,154 releases whose measured limiting corridor opened, the current-only minimum-norm carrier would instead close that corridor in 312. The fraction rose to $15/19$ among opening active handoffs and $12/12$ among active handoffs that also had no positive sector-speed derivative. For those 12 jointly guarded releases, the minimum carrier reproducing both measured current and measured corridor rate used $80.5\%$--$85.8\%$ of the actual collective rate norm. By orthogonality, the remaining four-dimensional joint-neutral norm fraction was $51.3\%$--$59.4\%$.

Plainly: the current/corridor conflict is not generic to every opening release, but it is present in every member of the sampled jointly guarded handoff class. Even after paying for current and corridor opening, roughly half the rate norm remains available for other geometric tasks.

The 12-row class includes eleven deliberately oversampled local neighbors, so the $12/12$ count is not a basin-frequency estimate. It identifies a coherent local motif whose next test is matched-history evolution of projector-built seeds.

The Euclidean projector minimizes $\|\mathbf Q\|$, but the causal speed guard depends on

$$
v_{\max}=\max(\|\mathbf q_+\|,\|\mathbf q_-\|).
$$

On the nondegenerate branch where both sector bounds are active, the exact minimax carrier is obtained from the weighted projector

$$
\mathbf Q_\lambda
=
W_\lambda^{-1}A^{\mathsf T}
(AW_\lambda^{-1}A^{\mathsf T})^{-1}\mathbf b,
\qquad
W_\lambda=\operatorname{diag}(I_3,\lambda I_3),
$$

with $\lambda>0$ chosen so $\|\mathbf q_+\|=\|\mathbf q_-\|$. For the selected shape and observed opening rate, the Euclidean carrier put maximum member speed $0.409796$ in one sector. The minimax carrier balanced both sectors at $0.341973$, below the actual release maximum $0.388663$. On the nonclosing boundary, the minimax speed was $0.280086$.

Plainly: the best way to save total motion can overload one sector. Balancing the two sector speeds gives a better causal-speed seed while preserving exactly the same current and corridor opening. This is the appropriate projector for future $c_f=1$-guarded seed construction.

The shared-phase harmonic family could not realize that projected rate at the same endpoint shape because its four breathing rates were locked to one common phase. Allowing an independent phase for each axial and radial breathing coordinate supplied an exact endpoint-matched harmonic history at the same $1.25$ breathing frequency. All four required amplitudes remained below $0.118$, both sector speeds reconstructed as $0.341973$, and all 64 release root rows certified under the strict $10^{-5}$ contract.

The same immutable history evolved without rejection to $T=0.28$. Positive cadence reversed near $T=0.194854$; maximum member speed stayed below $0.403658$; total current retained $86.24\%$ of its release magnitude; and root-time pressure reached $0.842014$. The limiting pair first opened, then turned and closed to $0.391664$ at rate $-0.420275$. Thus the minimax projector produces a causally realizable, lower-speed current handoff, but one instantaneous corridor constraint does not supply a self-regulating return.

Plainly: separate breathing phases are genuine geometric control coordinates, not decorative fit parameters. They can realize the desired current and opening motion without driving either sector near $c_f=1$. What is still missing is a delayed feedback cycle that rotates the four joint-neutral directions as the most dangerous pair changes.

The bounded release census evaluated 2,311 distinct root-certified F6c release records with no analyzer failure. Of these, 1,129 had a nonclosing limiting pair corridor and 394 also had nonincreasing member speed in both sectors. Adding $\|\dot{\mathbf q}_\sigma\|$ exposed many false-safe rows whose speed was decreasing only because a large acceleration was bending the rate vector.

The lowest-curvature member of that subset began with minimum pair distance $0.563903$, opening rate $0.276294$, maximum member speed $0.329593$, and maximum $\|\dot{\mathbf q}_\sigma\|=1.04193$. It evolved with no rejected step to $T=0.40$, retained the exact F6c surface below $8\times10^{-16}$ normalized velocity residual, and still had minimum pair distance $0.539185$. Its current, however, fell from $-0.611170$ to $-0.095242$ as one sector moved near a current-null direction. Geometric survival did not preserve current.

Plainly: the smoothest safe-looking motion stayed geometrically healthy but lost most of its field-facing current. A viable current braid needs an additional transport condition.

The strongest current-flow release instead had

$$
(I_+,I_-)=(-0.128256,-0.594811),
$$

$$
(\dot I_+,\dot I_-)=(-0.437531,+0.434729),
\qquad
\dot I_{\mathrm{tot}}=-0.00280243.
$$

Thus its nearly stationary total current came from rapid, opposed transfer between polarity sectors rather than two individually stationary currents. An independent $\Delta T=10^{-4}$ EOM continuation measured sector slopes $-0.437548$ and $+0.434524$. On the full continuation through $T=0.240$, total current changed only from $-0.723067$ to $-0.743349$ while the sector currents changed to $-0.222165$ and $-0.521184$. The F6c surface remained invariant below $8.4\times10^{-16}$ normalized velocity residual.

Plainly: one promising architecture clue is conjugate current handoff. One sub-braid can take over current support as the other gives it up, keeping the assembly-level current nearly constant without freezing either sub-braid.

The handoff is selective but not unique in the bounded archive. After deduplicating diagnostic replays and tolerance controls by normalized release geometry and coupling, 2,299 distinct releases remained. Of these, 1,079 had opposed sector-current derivatives and 190 canceled at least 90% of their absolute sector-derivative sum. A declared active-handoff screen required

$$
1-
\frac{|\dot I_++\dot I_-|}{|\dot I_+|+|\dot I_-|}
\ge 0.9,

$$
\frac{|\dot I_+|+|\dot I_-|}{|I_++I_-|}
\ge 0.5,
\qquad
\frac{|\dot I_++\dot I_-|}{|I_++I_-|}
\le 0.1.
$$

Twenty-nine releases met all three conditions. Many nevertheless began with a closing pair corridor. Current handoff is therefore a recurring but selective F6c motion class, not a sufficient survival condition.

The class does not reduce to a simple amplitude-sign recipe. Opposite axial- breathing signs occurred in $1{,}664/2{,}299=72.4\%$ of the full archive and $23/29=79.3\%$ of active handoffs. Opposite radial-breathing signs occurred in $1{,}450/2{,}299=63.1\%$ and $20/29=69.0\%$, respectively. The modest enrichment is descriptive only because the archive was not sampled to give every sign pattern equal measure.

Corridor motion showed the larger bounded contrast. Overall, $1{,}121/2{,}299=48.8\%$ of releases had a nonclosing limiting pair, versus only $8/29=27.6\%$ of active handoffs. Only one active-handoff release was both opening and nonaccelerating in both sectors. In this sampled family, rapid current exchange often accompanies compression or another unsafe flow.

Plainly: opposite breathing is a useful ingredient but not the handoff rule. The actual rule is a coordinated cancellation among shape, motion, and acceleration. It must be solved directly while corridor safety is enforced as a separate condition.

Plainly: the handoff was not manufactured by one lucky row. The archive contains a small family of it, but most members still fail other geometric guards. That makes handoff a useful search coordinate rather than a solution.

A focused $7\times7$ screen then varied only negative-sector phase and the common breathing-cycle phase around the one release that passed current, corridor, and speed-flow guards. All 49 requested releases certified; 12 met the active-handoff screen and 11 also had a nonclosing limiting pair plus no positive sector-speed derivative. Its current magnitude per unit maximum member speed was $1.87364$.

Plainly: the jointly guarded handoff is a local phase-space corridor, not a single tuned point. Changing only two phases produced eleven nearby releases with the same immediate qualitative safeguards.

Including those 49 local releases expanded the deduplicated census to 2,348 geometries. The median current-direction efficiency

=

was $0.4180$ across the full archive and $0.5205$ among 41 active handoffs. These distributions are descriptive because the local screen deliberately oversamples one phase neighborhood.

That record did not establish retention. A matched-input tolerance control then changed only root-time tolerance from $10^{-5}$ to $2\times10^{-5}$. 0.296899.

The tangential term was only $0.13\%$ of the sector-current magnitude and slightly opposed the breathing terms. This is an exact decomposition of the F6c current functional on the measured frame, not a decomposition of a recovered magnetic field.

Plainly: a current-supporting braid need not consist of permanently same-sense circular motion. One sector can stop and reverse its orbital cadence while coordinated breathing and the conjugate sector continue to carry almost the same total current. This sharpens the frequency inference used in Standard Model braid searches. A required inverse-time scale implies regular or recurrent motion, but not necessarily an always-forward orbit. Breathing, circulation, sector handoff, and observable-specific return order can share one mixed delayed-history cycle. Candidate generators should therefore admit cadence stops and reversals rather than rejecting them as loss of the current channel.

The best neighbor from the focused screen reproduced the cadence turn under the tighter $10^{-5}$ root-time contract. The reversed tangential term opposed the two breathing terms. At release this history had $\eta_I=0.625900$ and $\|\mathbf Q_\perp\|/\|\mathbf Q\|=0.7799$. At $T=0.290$ the corresponding values were $0.585280$ and $0.8108$. Current persistence therefore coexisted with increasing current-neutral motion rather than convergence toward the minimum-norm carrier.

The neutral motion also changed type. At release, $76.82\%$ of $\|\mathbf Q_\perp\|^2$ was tangential and $23.18\%$ was axial plus radial. At the first sampled frame after the cadence crossing, $T=0.2525$, only $0.83\%$ was tangential and $99.17\%$ was breathing. At $T=0.290$, breathing still supplied $94.52\%$ of the current-neutral squared norm. This is a measured mode conversion within the exact orthogonal decomposition: the neutral reservoir rotates from circulation-dominated to breathing-dominated motion as one sector reverses.

Plainly: the unused motion does not disappear when the orbit stops. It moves into axial and radial breathing. Candidate braid generators should allow this kind of internal mode conversion rather than treating orbital, breathing, and current motions as separate fixed channels.

Four additional strict-contract histories tested the local extent and event ordering. Three shared breathing-cycle phase $1.720796$ while varying the negative-sector phase through $1.446263$, $1.496263$, and $1.546263$. All three crossed positive cadence zero at

$$

and remained certified afterward until $T=(0.2850,0.2900,0.2975)$. Their terminal root-time pressures were $(0.99669,0.99034,0.98161)$, with finite pair distances $(0.39391,0.38873,0.38172)$ and maximum member speeds $(0.50803,0.52008,0.53729)$. Increasing the sampled negative-sector phase therefore moved cadence zero earlier and the certification boundary later, expanding the certified post-turn interval from about $0.0300$ to $0.0477$. This is a local trend, not a monotonicity theorem.

Linear interpolation of collective coordinates to $\dot\theta_+=0$ exposed a candidate shared section. For the three equal-cycle-phase histories, total current lay in

$$
-0.634423\le I_{\mathrm{tot}}\le-0.634098,
$$

a relative span of $0.051\%$, even though the positive and negative sector currents shifted oppositely. Current efficiency lay between $0.65167$ and $0.65568$, and tangential motion supplied only $0.8005\%$--$0.8280\%$ of the current-neutral squared norm. The efficiency and neutral-norm fraction are not independent agreements: the exact orthogonal decomposition imposes $\|\mathbf Q_\perp\|/\|\mathbf Q\|=\sqrt{1-\eta_I^2}$.

Changing breathing-cycle phase to $1.670796$ still produced cadence zero, but the section current shifted to $-0.674331$. Thus the near-fixed current is specific to the equal-cycle-phase slice, whereas the approximately fixed efficiency and breathing-dominated neutral mode persisted across the four turning histories.

The mode conversion preceded the cadence turn. Across five nearby strict histories, the first sampled frame with tangential neutral fraction at most $1\%$ occurred at $T=0.215$--$0.220$ while positive cadence was still $0.184$--$0.235$. In the four histories that later turned, the sampled conversion led cadence zero by $0.0347$--$0.0409$. The fifth history, at breathing-cycle phase $1.620796$, converted by $T=0.220$ but reached its root-enclosure boundary at $T=0.2525$ with positive cadence still $0.07362$.

Plainly: mode conversion is the more robust event. The internal motion first becomes almost entirely breathing-neutral while circulation is still forward; only later does the orbital cadence stop. Phase controls whether the delayed history remains certifiable long enough to complete that reversal.

Plainly: the stricter record now confirms the qualitative clue. Orbital motion can stop and reverse without switching off the sector current, because axial and radial breathing take over. What remains open is recurrence: the record ends at its root-enclosure certification wall before any complete return.

The resulting geometry requirement is joint:

1. conjugate sectors may exchange current contribution while keeping $\dot I_{\mathrm{tot}}$ small;
2. each sector must retain adequate same-polarity causal-history spacing;
3. opposite-polarity spatial corridors must remain open or turn before collapse; and
4. the combined motion must preserve field-speed margin and the exact identity action through a complete return; and
5. certified root-time enclosure pressure must remain controlled under tolerance and history-enclosure refinement.

Claim grade: the current and current-flow identities are derived on the F6c chart; census counts, threshold classes, and continuations are measured EOM- solver diagnostics on bounded declared records, not an independent retained- braid result. The active-handoff thresholds are declared diagnostic cuts, not natural constants.
